\displaystyle+C_{*}\lVert\sigma u_{tt}\rVert_{L^{\infty}((0,T),L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla u_{t},\,F_{t},\,\lambda_{t}\dvg u,\,\mu_{t}\mathbb{D}u\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\tfrac{8}{d}-2}.

This ends the proof of (
3.10
) and we turn to the last a priori estimate.
∎

Proof of (
3.11
)
.

Here, we translate the bounds of
u_{t}
into a piecewise Hölder regularity for the velocity gradient. To achieve this, we will use the results from
appendix

A
to derive a piecewise Hölder estimate for the discontinuous terms in the expression of the velocity gradient (
3.21
). We fix
q=2d/\alpha
.

Step 1: Terms involving
F

We estimate the terms

\frac{1}{\widetilde{\mu}}(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla\mathcal{P}\dvg F\quad\text{and}\quad\nabla^{2}(-\Delta)^{-1}\left[\dfrac{1}{2\widetilde{\mu}+\lambda}(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg F\right].

With the help of (
A.2
) and (
A.3
), we have:

(3.33)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\vskip 5.69046pt\lVert(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla^{2}F,\;(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla\mathcal{P}\dvg F\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}&\leqslant C\left(\ell_{\varphi_{0}}^{-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}}\lVert F\rVert_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\lVert F\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right),\\
\lVert(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla^{2}F,\;(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla\mathcal{P}\dvg F\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}&\leqslant C(1+\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}})\lVert F\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\end{cases}

where
\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}
is defined in (
1.4
). Similarly, we have:

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{2}(-\Delta)^{-1}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left[\dfrac{1}{2\widetilde{\mu}+\lambda}(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg F\right]\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left[\left(\lVert\lambda\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\ell_{\varphi_{0}}^{-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}}\right)\lVert F\rVert_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\lVert(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg F\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left[\left(\lVert\lambda\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\ell_{\varphi_{0}}^{-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}}\right)\lVert F\rVert_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+(1+\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}})\lVert F\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right],

and

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{2}(-\Delta)^{-1}\left[\dfrac{1}{2\widetilde{\mu}+\lambda}(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg F\right]\right\|_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\lVert(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg F\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle+C\left(\lVert\lambda\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}\right)\lVert(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg F\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(1+\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}+\lVert\lambda\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)\lVert F\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle+C\left(\lVert\lambda\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}\right)\ell_{\varphi_{0}}^{-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}}\lVert F\rVert_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

In sum, we have:

\displaystyle\left\|(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla\mathcal{P}\dvg F,\right.

\displaystyle\left.\;\nabla^{2}(-\Delta)^{-1}\left[\dfrac{1}{2\widetilde{\mu}+\lambda}(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg F\right]\right\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

(3.34)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(1+\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}+\lVert\lambda\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)\left(\ell_{\varphi_{0}}^{-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}}\lVert F\rVert_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\lVert F\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right).

Step 2: Small terms

We estimate the terms

\frac{1}{\widetilde{\mu}}(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla\mathcal{P}\dvg\{2(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}u\}\quad\text{and}\quad\nabla^{2}(-\Delta)^{-1}\left[\dfrac{1}{2\widetilde{\mu}+\lambda}(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg\{2(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}u\}\right].

Again, with the help of (
A.2
), we have:

\displaystyle\lVert(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla^{2}\{2(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}u\}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(\lVert\mu\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\ell_{\varphi_{0}}^{-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}}\lVert\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

(3.35)

\displaystyle+C\lVert\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Next, we gather
A.2
and (
A.3
) to obtain:

\displaystyle\lVert(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla^{2}(w)\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\lVert(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla^{2}(w^{e})\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\lVert(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla^{2}(w-w^{e})\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left[\lVert w^{e}\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\lVert w-w^{e}\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}\lVert w-w^{e}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left[\lVert w\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\left(\ell^{-\alpha}_{\varphi_{0}}+\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}\right)\lVert\llbracket w\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}\right].

Therefore:

(3.36)

\displaystyle\lVert(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla^{2}\{2(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}u\}\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant C\left[\lVert(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}u\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\left(\ell^{-\alpha}_{\varphi_{0}}+\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}\right)\lVert\llbracket(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}u\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}\right].

Similarly, we have:

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{2}(-\Delta)^{-1}\vphantom{\dfrac{1}{2\widetilde{\mu}+\lambda}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left[\dfrac{1}{2\widetilde{\mu}+\lambda}(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg\{2(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}u\}\right]\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(\lVert\lambda\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\ell_{\varphi_{0}}^{-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}}\right)\lVert\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+C\lVert(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg\{2(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}u\}\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left[\lVert\mu\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\left(\lVert\lambda\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\ell_{\varphi_{0}}^{-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}}\right)\lVert\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right]\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

(3.37)

\displaystyle+C\left(\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}+\ell^{-\alpha}_{\varphi_{0}}\right)\lVert\llbracket(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}u\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}+C\lVert\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

and

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{2}(-\Delta)^{-1}\vphantom{\dfrac{1}{2\widetilde{\mu}+\lambda}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left[\dfrac{1}{2\widetilde{\mu}+\lambda}(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg\{2(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}u\}\right]\right\|_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left\|\dfrac{1}{2\widetilde{\mu}+\lambda}(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg\{2(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}u\}\right\|_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle+C\left(\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}+\ell^{-\alpha}_{\varphi_{0}}\right)\left\|\llbracket\dfrac{1}{2\widetilde{\mu}+\lambda}(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg\{2(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}u\}\rrbracket\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(\lVert\lambda\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\left(\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}+\ell^{-\alpha}_{\varphi_{0}}\right)\lVert\llbracket\lambda\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}\right)\lVert(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg\{2(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}u\}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle+C\left(\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}+\ell^{-\alpha}_{\varphi_{0}}\right)\left\|\llbracket(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg\{2(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}u\}\rrbracket\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}

\displaystyle+C\left\|(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg\{2(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}u\}\right\|_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(\lVert\lambda\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\left(\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}+\ell^{-\alpha}_{\varphi_{0}}\right)\lVert\llbracket\lambda\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}\right)\left(\lVert\mu\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\ell_{\varphi_{0}}^{-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}}\lVert\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle+C\left[1+\lVert\lambda\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\left(\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}+\ell^{-\alpha}_{\varphi_{0}}\right)\lVert\llbracket\lambda\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}\right]\lVert\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

(3.38)

\displaystyle+C\left(\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}+\ell^{-\alpha}_{\varphi_{0}}\right)\left\|\llbracket(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg\{2(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}u\}\rrbracket,\;\llbracket(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}u\rrbracket\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}.

We now turn to estimating the norms of the jumps involved in the above inequalities. We first notice that the solution
u
of (
3.1
) satisfies:

(3.39)

\displaystyle\llbracket 2\mu\mathbb{D}^{jk}u+\lambda\dvg u\delta^{jk}+F^{jk}\rrbracket n_{x}^{k}=0,

where
n_{x}
is the normal vector field of
\mathcal{C}_{0}
and
\delta^{jk}
denotes the Kronecker symbol. We consider an unit vector field
\tau_{x,l}
,
l\in\llbracket 1,d-1\rrbracket
such that
(\tau_{x,1},\dots,\,\tau_{x,d-1},\,n_{x})
forms a basis of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Given that
u
is continuous in the whole space and
\nabla u
is continuous on both sides of
\mathcal{C}_{0}
,
then discontinuity in
\nabla u
may appear only in the normal direction of
\mathcal{C}_{0}
. Namely, we have:

\forall\,l\in\llbracket 1,d-1\rrbracket,\quad\llbracket\nabla u\rrbracket\cdot\tau_{x,l}=0

and there exists a vector field
\vec{\mathrm{a}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that:

(3.40)

\displaystyle\llbracket\nabla u\rrbracket=\vec{\mathrm{a}}\otimes n_{x}.

If
\langle g\rangle
denotes the average of
g
at the interface, then we rewrite (
3.39
) as

2\langle\mu\rangle\llbracket\mathbb{D}^{jk}u\rrbracket n_{x}^{k}+\langle\lambda\rangle\llbracket\dvg u\rrbracket n_{x}^{j}=-2\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\langle\mathbb{D}^{jk}u\rangle n_{x}^{k}-\llbracket\lambda\rrbracket\langle\dvg u\rangle n_{x}^{j}-\llbracket F^{jk}\rrbracket n_{x}^{k},

and scalar products with
n_{x}
and
\tau_{x,l}
together with (
3.40
) lead to:

(3.41)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\langle 2\mu+\lambda\rangle\vec{\mathrm{a}}\cdot n_{x}=-2\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\langle\mathbb{D}^{jk}u\rangle n_{x}^{k}n_{x}^{j}-\llbracket\lambda\rrbracket\langle\dvg u\rangle-\llbracket F^{jk}\rrbracket n_{x}^{k}n_{x}^{j},\\
\langle\mu\rangle\vec{\mathrm{a}}\cdot\tau_{x,l}=-2\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\langle\mathbb{D}^{jk}u\rangle n_{x}^{k}\tau_{x,l}^{j}-\llbracket F^{jk}\rrbracket n_{x}^{k}\tau_{x,l}^{j}.\end{cases}

Therefore:

\displaystyle\llbracket(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}u\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\langle\mathbb{D}u\rangle+\langle\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\rangle\llbracket\mathbb{D}u\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\langle\mathbb{D}u\rangle+\dfrac{\langle\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\rangle}{\langle\mu\rangle}\langle\mu\rangle\llbracket\mathbb{D}u\rrbracket

and recalling (
3.40
), (
3.41
) with

\vec{\mathrm{a}}=\vec{\mathrm{a}}\cdot n_{x}n_{x}+\sum_{l}\vec{\mathrm{a}}\cdot\tau_{x,l}\tau_{x,l},

we infer:

\displaystyle\lVert\llbracket(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}u\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{*}\lVert F\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+C\lVert\llbracket\lambda\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}\left\|\dfrac{\langle\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\rangle}{\langle\mu\rangle}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

(3.42)

\displaystyle+C\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}\left(1+\left\|\dfrac{\langle\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\rangle}{\langle\mu\rangle}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}\right)\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Moreover, from (
3.20
) and
\eqref{c3.42}_{1}
, we find (recall
\llbracket\dvg u\rrbracket=a\cdot n_{x}
):

\displaystyle\llbracket(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg\{(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}u\}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket(2\widetilde{\mu}+\lambda)\dvg u\rrbracket-\llbracket(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg F\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\langle 2\widetilde{\mu}+\lambda\rangle}{\langle 2\mu+\lambda\rangle}\langle 2\mu+\lambda\rangle\llbracket\dvg u\rrbracket+\llbracket\lambda\rrbracket\langle\dvg u\rangle-\llbracket(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg F\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\langle 2\widetilde{\mu}+\lambda\rangle}{\langle 2\mu+\lambda\rangle}\left(-2\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\langle\mathbb{D}^{jk}u\rangle n_{x}^{k}n_{x}^{j}-\llbracket F^{jk}\rrbracket n_{x}^{k}n_{x}^{j}\right)

\displaystyle+2\dfrac{\langle\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\rangle}{\langle 2\mu+\lambda\rangle}\llbracket\lambda\rrbracket\langle\dvg u\rangle-\llbracket(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg F\rrbracket

and hence (recall (
3.33
)):

\displaystyle\lVert\llbracket(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg

\displaystyle\{(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}u\}\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}\leqslant C\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}\left(1+\left\|\dfrac{\langle\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\rangle}{\langle\mu\rangle}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}\right)\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

(3.43)

\displaystyle+C\lVert\llbracket\lambda\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}\left\|\dfrac{\langle\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\rangle}{\langle\mu\rangle}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+C\left(\ell_{\varphi_{0}}^{-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}}\lVert F\rVert_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\lVert F\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right).

Summing up (
3.35
), (
3.36
), (
3.37
), (
3.38
), (
3.42
) and (
3.43
) we find:

\displaystyle\left\|(-\Delta)^{-1}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\nabla\mathcal{P}\vphantom{\dfrac{1}{2\widetilde{\mu}+\lambda}}\dvg\{(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}u\},\;\nabla^{2}(-\Delta)^{-1}\left[\dfrac{1}{2\widetilde{\mu}+\lambda}(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg\{(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}u\}\right]\right\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{**}\left(\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\lVert F\rVert_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\lVert F\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)+C\left(\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}+\ell^{-\alpha}_{\varphi_{0}}\right)\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle+C\left(1+\lVert\lambda\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\left(\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}+\ell^{-\alpha}_{\varphi_{0}}\right)\lVert\llbracket\lambda\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}\right)\lVert\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

(3.44)

\displaystyle+C\left(\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}+\ell^{-\alpha}_{\varphi_{0}}\right)\lVert\llbracket\lambda,\;\mu\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}\left\|\dfrac{\langle\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\rangle}{\langle\mu\rangle}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

Above, the constant
C_{**}
depends polynomially on the lower and upper bounds of the viscosity and density, on
\displaystyle\sup_{[0,T]}\lVert\mu,\,\lambda\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
, and on
\ell^{-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}}_{\varphi_{0}}
and
\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}
.

Step 3: Remaining terms

We estimate the terms

\frac{1}{\widetilde{\mu}}(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla\mathcal{P}\left(\rho_{0}u_{t}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\nabla^{2}(-\Delta)^{-1}\left[\dfrac{1}{2\widetilde{\mu}+\lambda}(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg(\rho_{0}u_{t})\right].

The first term above is Hölder continuous in the whole space, given the regularity previously obtained for
u_{t}
. Indeed,
by the interpolation inequality and (
3.22
):

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{\tfrac{d\beta}{4}}\lVert(-\Delta)^{-1}

\displaystyle\nabla\mathcal{P}(\rho_{0}u_{t});\,(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla(\rho_{0}u_{t})\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{4}\leqslant C\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{\tfrac{d\beta}{4}}\lVert\rho_{0}u_{t}\rVert_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{4\beta}\lVert\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}(\rho_{0}u_{t})\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{4(1-\beta)}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{*}\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{\tfrac{d\beta}{4}}\lVert\nabla u_{t}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{d\beta}\lVert u_{t}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{(4-d)\beta}\lVert F,\nabla u\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{4(1-\beta)}

(3.45)

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{*}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\sigma\lVert\nabla u_{t}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{4}\right]^{\tfrac{d\beta}{4}}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\lVert u_{t}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{4}\right]^{(1-\tfrac{d}{4})\beta}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\lVert F,\;\nabla u\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{4}\right]^{1-\beta}

with

\beta=\dfrac{2d}{4+d}.

Given that
\dot{W}^{1,\tfrac{d}{1-\alpha}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we have:

(3.46)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{r}\lVert(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla\mathcal{P}(\rho_{0}u_{t});\,(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla(\rho_{0}u_{t})\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{4}\leqslant C_{*}\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{r}\lVert u_{t}\rVert_{L^{d/(1-\alpha)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{4}.

For
d=2
, we have:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{\alpha}\lVert u_{t}\rVert_{L^{2/(1-\alpha)}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{4}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{\alpha}\lVert\nabla u_{t}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{4\alpha}\lVert u_{t}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{4(1-\alpha)}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left[\int_{0}^{T}\sigma\lVert\nabla u_{t}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))}^{4}\right]^{\alpha}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\lVert u_{t}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{4}\right]^{1-\alpha}

whereas for
d=3
, we only have for all
0<\alpha<1/2

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{2\alpha}\lVert u_{t}\rVert_{L^{3/(1-\alpha)}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{4}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{2\alpha}\lVert\nabla u_{t}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2(1+2\alpha)}\lVert u_{t}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2(1-2\alpha)}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\sup_{[0,T]}\lVert u_{t}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2(1-2\alpha)}\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{2\alpha}\lVert\nabla u_{t}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2(1+2\alpha)}.

For higher regularity,
1/2\leqslant\alpha<1
, we have:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{\tfrac{2}{3}(5\alpha-1)}\lVert u_{t}\rVert_{L^{3/(1-\alpha)}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{4}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{\tfrac{2}{3}(5\alpha-1)}\lVert\nabla u_{t}\rVert_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{\tfrac{16}{3}(\alpha-\tfrac{1}{2})}\lVert\nabla u_{t}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{\tfrac{4}{3}(5-4\alpha)}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left[\lVert\sqrt{\sigma}\nabla u_{t}\rVert_{L^{\infty}((0,T),L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))}^{2}\right]^{\tfrac{2}{3}(2-\alpha)}

\displaystyle\times\left[\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{2}\lVert\nabla u_{t}\rVert_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{8/3}\right]^{2\alpha-1}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla u_{t}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right]^{2(1-\alpha)}.

In conclusion, choosing
r
as:

(3.47)

\displaystyle r=\begin{cases}\max\left(1/3,\,2\alpha\right)\quad&\text{if}\quad d=2,\\
\max\left(\dfrac{9}{14},\,2\alpha\right)\quad&\text{if}\quad d=3,\;\alpha\in(0,1/2)\\
\dfrac{2}{3}(5\alpha-1)\quad&\text{if }\quad d=3,\;\alpha\in[1/2,1).\end{cases}

we have, owing to Young’s inequality:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{r}\lVert(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla\mathcal{P}\left(\rho_{0}u_{t}\right),

\displaystyle\;(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla\left(\rho_{0}u_{t}\right)\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{4}\leqslant C_{*}\lVert F,\,\nabla u,\,u_{t},\,\sqrt{\sigma}\nabla u_{t}\rVert_{L^{\infty}((0,T),L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}^{4}

(3.48)

\displaystyle+C_{*}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\lVert F,\,\nabla u,\,u_{t},\,\nabla u_{t}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\right]^{2}+C_{*}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{2}\lVert\nabla u_{t}\rVert_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{8/d}\right]^{d/2}.

The second term is discontinuous at the interface, and we have:

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{2}(-\Delta)^{-1}\left[\dfrac{1}{2\widetilde{\mu}+\lambda}(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg(\rho_{0}u_{t})\right]\right\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(\lVert\lambda\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\ell_{\varphi_{0}}^{-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}}\right)\lVert(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla(\rho_{0}u_{t})\rVert_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle+\left(1+\lVert\lambda\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}\right)\lVert(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla(\rho_{0}u_{t})\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Finally, we interpolate the
L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
norm above between
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and we recall (
3.48
) to obtain:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{r}\left\|\frac{1}{\widetilde{\mu}}(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla\mathcal{P}\left(\rho_{0}u_{t}\right),\,\right.

\displaystyle\left.\,\nabla^{2}(-\Delta)^{-1}\left[\dfrac{1}{2\widetilde{\mu}+\lambda}(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg(\rho_{0}u_{t})\right]\right\|^{4}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{**}\lVert F,\,\nabla u,\,u_{t},\,\sqrt{\sigma}\nabla u_{t}\rVert_{L^{\infty}((0,T),L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}^{4}

(3.49)

\displaystyle+C_{**}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\lVert F,\,\nabla u,\,u_{t},\,\nabla u_{t}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\right]^{2}+C_{**}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{2}\lVert\nabla u_{t}\rVert_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{8/d}\right]^{d/2}.

Step 4: Closing of all estimates

It follows from (
3.34
), (
3.44
) and (
3.49
) that:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{r}

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{4}\leqslant C_{**}\lVert F,\,\nabla u,\,u_{t},\,\sqrt{\sigma}\nabla u_{t}\rVert_{L^{\infty}((0,T),L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}^{4}+C_{**}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\lVert F,\,\nabla u,\,u_{t},\,\nabla u_{t}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle+C_{**}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{2}\lVert\nabla u_{t}\rVert_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{8/d}\right]^{d/2}+C_{**}\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{r}\left(\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{4}+\lVert F\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{4}\right)

\displaystyle+C\left(\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}+\ell^{-\alpha}_{\varphi_{0}}\right)^{4}\sup_{[0,T]}\left[\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}^{4}+\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket,\,\llbracket\lambda\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}^{4}\left\|\dfrac{\langle\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\rangle}{\langle\mu\rangle}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}^{4}\right]\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{r}\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{4}

\displaystyle+C\sup_{[0,T]}\left[1+\lVert\lambda\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\left(\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}+\ell^{-\alpha}_{\varphi_{0}}\right)\lVert\llbracket\lambda\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}\right]^{4}\sup_{[0,T]}\lVert\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{4}\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{r}\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{4}.

Therefore there exists a constant
[\mu]>0
depending only on
d,\alpha
and
\widetilde{\mu}
such that if:

\displaystyle\sup_{[0,T]}\left[1+\lVert\lambda\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\left(\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}+\ell^{-\alpha}_{\varphi_{0}}\right)\lVert\llbracket\lambda\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}\right]\sup_{[0,T]}\lVert\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

(3.50)

\displaystyle+\left(\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}+\ell^{-\alpha}_{\varphi_{0}}\right)\sup_{[0,T]}\left[\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}+\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket,\,\llbracket\lambda\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}\left\|\dfrac{\langle\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\rangle}{\langle\mu\rangle}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}\right]\leqslant[\mu],

then the last two terms can be absorbed in the left hand side and interpolating the
L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
norm of the velocity gradient between
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
followed by Young’s inequality, we find (
3.11
).
∎This completes the first part of the proof of
3.1
concerning the a priori estimates. We now move to the second part in the proof of
3.1
, focusing on the homotopy argument.

For
\theta\in[0,1]
, we define:

\mu^{\theta}=(1-\theta)\widetilde{\mu}+\theta\mu,\quad\text{ and }\quad\lambda^{\theta}=(1-\theta)\widetilde{\lambda}+\theta\lambda

and we consider (
3.6
) where the viscosities
\mu,\,\lambda
are replaced by
\mu^{\theta}
and
\lambda^{\theta}
respectively :

(3.51)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\rho_{0}u_{t}-\dvg(2\mu^{\theta}\mathbb{D}u+\lambda^{\theta}\dvg uI_{d})=\dvg F,\\
u_{|t=0}=u_{0}.\end{cases}

In order to ease the reading, we set
Y_{T}^{\theta}:=Y_{T}(\mu^{\theta}_{0},\lambda^{\theta}_{0})
. The principle of homotopy argument is to look for
\theta\in[0,1]
such that (
3.51
) is uniquely solvable in
X_{T}
. Therefore, we define the following set:

(3.52)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}:=\big\{\theta\in[0,1]\colon\forall(F,u_{0})\in Y_{T}^{\theta},\,\exists\,!\;u\in X_{T}\text{ solution of }\eqref{eq3.19}\big\}.

It is obvious that
3.1
follows as soon as
1\in\mathscr{E}
. This will be obtained by proving that
\mathscr{E}
is at the same time a non-empty, open and closed set of
[0,1]
.
The proof is structured in two subsections: In the first one (see
section

3.2.2
), we prove that
0\in\mathscr{E}
. In the second one (see
section

3.2.3
), we show that
\mathscr{E}
is both an open and a closed subset of
[0,1]
.

3.2.2.
Proof of
\mathscr{E}\neq\emptyset

Sketch of the proof

The aim is to prove that
0\in\mathscr{E}
.
When
\theta=0
, the equations (
3.51
) reduces to:

(3.53)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\rho_{0}u_{t}-\widetilde{\mu}\Delta u-(\widetilde{\mu}+\widetilde{\lambda})\nabla\dvg u=\dvg F,\\
u_{|t=0}=u_{0}\end{cases}

and the purpose is to prove that for all
(F,u_{0})\in Y_{T}^{0}
there exists a unique solution of (
3.53
) in
X_{T}
.
Solving (
3.53
) is not part of the classical theory for linear parabolic systems, as the initial density in front of
\partial_{t}u
is rough. Despite, one can add

(\widetilde{\rho}-\rho_{0})u_{t}
on both sides of (
3.53
), in order to obtain a heat equation with a right hand side that belongs to
L^{2}((0,T),L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))+L^{2}((0,T),\dot{H}^{-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
. As in
[
6
]
, we may require small fluctuation of the density.
In order to get around this difficulty, the strategy adopted is that, we consider a mollifying sequence of the initial density
\rho_{0}
, velocity
u_{0}
and
F
respectively
\rho_{0,\delta}

u_{0,\delta}
, and
F_{\delta}

and consider the equation:

(3.54)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\rho_{0,\delta}u_{t}-\widetilde{\mu}\Delta u-(\widetilde{\mu}+\widetilde{\lambda})\nabla\dvg u=\dvg F_{\delta},\\
u_{|t=0}=u_{0,\delta}.\end{cases}

The existence and uniqueness of global solution
u^{\delta}
of the above system is based on, a change of unknowns, a study of the heat kernel and a homotopy argument. Taking the advantage that for any
\delta>0
,
\rho_{0,\delta}
is smooth enough and bounded away from vacuum, one has that
u
solves (
3.54
) if and only if
v:=\rho_{0,\delta}u
solves the following Cauchy problem:

(3.55)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}v_{t}-\dvg\left(\dfrac{\widetilde{\mu}}{\rho_{0,\delta}}\mathbb{D}v\right)-\nabla\left(\dfrac{\widetilde{\lambda}}{\rho_{0,\delta}}\dvg v\right)&=\dvg F_{\delta}-\widetilde{\mu}\dvg\left(\rho_{0,\delta}^{-2}\left(\nabla\rho_{0,\delta}\otimes v+v\otimes\nabla\rho_{0,\delta}\right)\right)\\
&-\widetilde{\lambda}\nabla\left(\rho_{0,\delta}^{-2}v\cdot\nabla\rho_{0,\delta}\right),\\
v_{|t=0}&=\rho_{0,\delta}u_{0,\delta}.\end{cases}

In order to ease computations, we consider the following problem for
\mu^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0},b_{0}=\mu^{\prime}_{0}(x),\lambda^{\prime}_{0}(x),b_{0}(x)

(3.56)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}v_{t}-\dvg(2\mu_{0}^{\prime}\mathbb{D}v+\lambda_{0}^{\prime}\dvg vI_{d})=f+\dvg(b_{0}\cdot v),\\
v_{|t=0}=v_{0}\end{cases}

and we have the following:

Theorem 3.3
.

Let
0<T<\infty
and
f\in L^{2}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
0<\mu_{*}\leqslant\mu_{0}^{\prime},\,\lambda_{0}^{\prime}\in W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
b_{0}\in W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and finally
v_{0}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then there exists a unique solution

v\in\mathscr{C}([0,T],H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
with
v_{t},\nabla^{2}v\in L^{2}((0,T),L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
for the system (
3.56
). Moreover, there exist constants
\Lambda,\,K>0
that depend on the norms of
\mu^{\prime}_{0},\,\lambda_{0}^{\prime},\,b_{0}
and the lower and upper bounds
\mu_{*},\mu^{*}
such that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-2\Lambda t}\left\{\lVert v(t)\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\lVert v_{t},\,\nabla v,\,\nabla^{2}v\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\right\}\leqslant K\left(\lVert v_{0}\rVert^{2}_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert f(s)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}ds\right).

The proof of
3.3
is the purpose of the Step below, and it implies immediately:

Lemma 3.4
.

For all
\delta>0
, there exists a unique classical solution
u^{\delta}
of the Cauchy problem (
3.54
). The sequence
(u^{\delta})_{\delta}
converges to
u\in X_{T}
as
\delta\to 0
, the unique solution of the Cauchy problem (
3.53
).

All these discussions establish that
\mathscr{E}
is a non-empty set. In the following, we present the proof of
3.3
and
3.4
.

Detailed proof of
3.3
and
3.4

We start with the proof of
3.3
.

Proof of
3.3
.

The proof of
3.3
is based on a homotopy argument. So, we define

{\mu_{0}^{\prime}}^{\theta}=(1-\theta)\widetilde{\mu}+\theta\mu_{0}^{\prime}\quad\text{and}\quad{\lambda_{0}^{\prime}}^{\theta}=(1-\theta)\widetilde{\lambda}+\theta\lambda_{0}^{\prime},

and we consider the equation (
3.56
) where the viscosity
\mu_{0}^{\prime},\lambda_{0}^{\prime}
are replaced by
{\mu_{0}^{\prime}}^{\theta},\,{\lambda_{0}^{\prime}}^{\theta}
respectively. Also, we define

X_{T}^{\prime}:=\{v\in\mathscr{C}([0,T],H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))\colon v_{t},\,\nabla^{2}v\in L^{2}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})\}

and the map:

\displaystyle\Pi_{\theta}\colon\quad

\displaystyle X_{T}^{\prime}\quad\to L^{2}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\displaystyle v\quad\quad\mapsto\left(v_{t}-\dvg(2{\mu_{0}^{\prime}}^{\theta}\mathbb{D}v+{\lambda_{0}^{\prime}}^{\theta}\dvg vI_{d})-\dvg(b_{0}\cdot v),\;v_{|t=0}\right)

and finally we consider the set:

\mathscr{E}^{\prime}:=\{\theta\in[0,1]\colon\Pi_{\theta}\text{ is one to one}\}.

Obviously, the map
\Pi_{\theta}
is well-defined and continuous, we will only have to show that
1\in\mathscr{E}^{\prime}
to conclude the
proof of
3.3
.
To obtain
0\in\mathscr{E}^{\prime}
, we observe that for
\theta=0
, the equation
\eqref{eq3.23}_{\theta}
reduces to:

(3.57)

\displaystyle v_{t}-\widetilde{\mu}\Delta v-(\widetilde{\mu}+\widetilde{\lambda})\nabla\dvg v=f+\dvg(b_{0}\cdot v).

The existence of a unique solution
v
of (
3.57
) is established in
appendix

B
, resulting in
0\in\mathscr{E}^{\prime}
.
The remainder of the proof of
3.3
consists in first deriving a priori estimates for the solution
v\in X^{\prime}_{T}
of (
3.56
). Then, we prove that
\mathscr{E}^{\prime}
is at the same time open and closed.

A priori estimates

We multiply (
3.56
) by
v
and integrate in time and space in order to obtain:

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2}\lVert v(t)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}2\mu_{0}^{\prime}\lvert\mathbb{D}v\rvert^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lambda_{0}^{\prime}\lvert\dvg v\rvert^{2}=\dfrac{1}{2}\lVert v_{0}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}fv-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}b_{0}\cdot v\nabla v

\displaystyle\leqslant\eta\int_{0}^{t}\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\dfrac{1}{2}\lVert v_{0}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\lVert f\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+C_{\eta}\int_{0}^{t}(1+\lVert b_{0}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2})\lVert v\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2})

for some positive constant
\eta
small related to the lower bound of the viscosity,
in such way that the first term of the right hand side can be absorbed in the left hand side resulting in:

(3.58)

\displaystyle\lVert v(t)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\lVert\nabla v\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant C_{*}\lVert v_{0}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+C_{*}\int_{0}^{t}\lVert f\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+C_{*}\int_{0}^{t}(1+\lVert b_{0}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2})\lVert v\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

where
C_{*}
depends only on the bounds of the viscosity.

Next, we use
\Delta v
as a test function in (
3.56
). The first term is:

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}v_{t}\Delta v=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla v_{t}\cdot\nabla v=-\dfrac{1}{2}\dfrac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lvert\nabla v\rvert^{2}

and the second one is:

\displaystyle-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{k}(\mu_{0}^{\prime}\mathbb{D}^{jk}v)\Delta v^{j}

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{j}(\lambda_{0}^{\prime}\dvg v)\Delta v^{j}=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mu_{0}^{\prime}\lvert\Delta v\rvert^{2}-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mu_{0}^{\prime}\partial_{j}\dvg v\Delta v^{j}-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{k}\mu_{0}^{\prime}\mathbb{D}^{jk}v\Delta v^{j}

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lambda_{0}^{\prime}\lvert\nabla\dvg v\rvert^{2}-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dvg v\nabla\lambda_{0}^{\prime}\cdot\nabla\dvg v

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mu_{0}^{\prime}\lvert\Delta v\rvert^{2}-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\mu_{0}^{\prime}+\lambda_{0}^{\prime})\lvert\nabla\dvg v\rvert^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dvg v\partial_{j}\mu_{0}^{\prime}\Delta v^{j}

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dvg v\nabla\mu_{0}^{\prime}\cdot\nabla\dvg v-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{k}\mu_{0}^{\prime}\mathbb{D}^{jk}v\Delta v^{j}-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dvg v\nabla\lambda_{0}^{\prime}\cdot\nabla\dvg v.

Gathering all of theses computations, we have:

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2}\dfrac{d}{dt}\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mu_{0}^{\prime}\lvert\Delta v\rvert^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\mu_{0}^{\prime}+\lambda_{0}^{\prime})\lvert\nabla\dvg v\rvert^{2}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f^{j}\Delta v^{j}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dvg(b_{0}\cdot v)\Delta v^{j}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dvg v\partial_{j}\mu_{0}^{\prime}\Delta v^{j}-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dvg v\nabla\mu_{0}^{\prime}\cdot\nabla\dvg v-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{k}\mu_{0}^{\prime}\mathbb{D}^{jk}v\Delta v^{j}-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dvg v\nabla\lambda_{0}^{\prime}\cdot\nabla\dvg v.

Thus, Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities yield:

(3.59)

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla v(t)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\lVert\Delta v\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\leqslant C_{*}\lVert\nabla v_{0}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+C_{*}\int_{0}^{t}\lVert f\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+C_{*}\int_{0}^{t}\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\lVert\nabla\mu_{0}^{\prime},\nabla\lambda_{0}^{\prime},b_{0},\nabla b_{0}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

Finally, we gather (
3.58
) and (
3.59
), and we make use of Grönwall’s lemma and (
3.56
) to obtain:

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-\Lambda t}\left\{\lVert v(t)\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\lVert v_{t},\,\nabla v,\,\nabla^{2}v\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\right\}\leqslant C_{*}\lVert v_{0}\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+C_{*}\int_{0}^{T}\lVert f\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

with

\Lambda=C_{*}(1+\lVert\nabla\mu_{0}^{\prime},\nabla\lambda_{0}^{\prime},b_{0},\nabla b_{0}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}).

This ends the computations of the a priori estimates and we turn to the homotopy argument.

Closing of the homotopy argument

In this section, we will show that
\mathscr{E}^{\prime}=[0,1]
. We have already showed that
\mathscr{E}^{\prime}
is not empty.
Also, we may notice that if
\Pi_{\theta}
is invertible then, from the previous a priori estimates,
\Pi_{\theta}^{-1}
is linear continuous with a norm that depends only on
\Lambda
and time
T
. In particular, we have:

K_{T}:=\sup_{\theta\in\mathscr{E}^{\prime}}\lVert\Pi_{\theta}^{-1}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}}<\infty.

Let us prove that
\mathscr{E}^{\prime}
is an open set. Let
\theta_{0}\in\mathscr{E}^{\prime}
, then,
\Pi_{\theta_{0}}
is a one to one map and one can write, for all
\theta\in[0,1]
,

\Pi_{\theta}=\Pi_{\theta_{0}}+(\theta-\theta_{0})(\Pi_{1}-\Pi_{0})=\Pi_{\theta_{0}}\left(I+(\theta-\theta_{0})\Pi_{\theta_{0}}^{-1}(\Pi_{1}-\Pi_{0})\right).

Thus, all
\theta
such that
2\lvert\theta-\theta_{0}\rvert\lVert\Pi_{\theta_{0}}^{-1}(\Pi_{1}-\Pi_{0})\rVert_{\mathcal{L}(X_{T}^{\prime})}<1
belongs to
\mathscr{E}^{\prime}
, therefore
\mathscr{E}^{\prime}
is an open set of
[0,1]
. We turn to the last part. Let
(\theta_{n})_{n}
be a sequence of element of
\mathscr{E}^{\prime}
that converges to some
\theta\in[0,1]
. As above, one can write, for some
n_{0}

\Pi_{\theta}=\Pi_{\theta_{n_{0}}}\left(I+(\theta-\theta_{n_{0}})\Pi_{\theta_{n_{0}}}^{-1}(\Pi_{1}-\Pi_{0})\right).

Since
K_{T}<\infty
, one can choose large
n_{0}
such that

2\lvert\theta-\theta_{n_{0}}\rvert K_{T}\lVert\Pi_{1}-\Pi_{0}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}}<1

thus
\Pi_{\theta}
is one to one as composition of two one to one operators. Therefore
\theta\in\mathscr{E}^{\prime}
and consequently
\mathscr{E}^{\prime}
is a closed set of
[0,1]
. One then concludes that
1\in\mathscr{E}^{\prime}
and
3.3
then follows.
∎

Proof of
3.4
.

One deduces from
3.3
, (
3.55
) and then
(
3.54
)
that the Cauchy problem (
3.54
) admits a unique solution
u^{\delta}\in\mathscr{C}([0,T],H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
with
u_{t}^{\delta},\nabla^{2}u^{\delta}\in L^{2}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Moreover, by differentiating (
3.54
) with respect to space, and noting that
\rho_{0}^{\delta}
and
u_{0}^{\delta}
are smooth, we obtain that for all
s\geqslant 2
,
u^{\delta}\in\mathscr{C}([0,T],H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})),
and
u_{t}\,,\nabla^{2}u^{\delta}\in L^{2}((0,T),H^{s-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
. In the same manner, we differentiate
(
3.54
) with respect to time, in order to obtain the equation satisfied by
u_{t}^{\delta}
. Of course,
F_{t}^{\delta}
is regular enough and the regularity
u_{t}^{\delta}\in L^{2}((0,T),H^{s-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
is adequate for the differentiating to make sense. Thus, applying again
3.3
one deduces from the previous discussion that
u_{t}^{\delta}\in\mathscr{C}([0,T],H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))

and
u_{tt}^{\delta},\nabla^{2}u_{t}^{\delta}\in L^{2}((0,T),H^{s-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
. This closes the existence of classical solution
of the Cauchy problem (
3.54
).

The sequence
(u^{\delta})_{\delta}
verifies the estimates for
u
,
u_{t}
, (in (
3.7
))
u_{tt}
(in (
3.9
)) and the
L^{p}
estimates in
3.1
for
u
and
u_{t}
, uniformly with respect to the regularisation parameter
\delta>0
with the exception of the piecewise Hölder estimate. Hence, one can let
\delta
go to zero in order to obtain a solution of the Cauchy problem (
3.53
). Since
(u^{\delta})_{\delta}
is bounded in
L^{\infty}((0,T),H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
and its time derivative
(u_{t}^{\delta})_{\delta}
is bounded in
L^{2}((0,T),H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
, it then turns out that
u\in\mathscr{C}([0,T],H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
. On the other hand, given that

u_{t}\in L^{\infty}((0,T),L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
and
\sigma u_{tt}\in L^{2}((0,T),L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
it immediately follows
u_{t}\in\mathscr{C}((0,T],L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
. It only remains the continuity of
u_{t}
at the initial time. To achieve this, we first observe that for all
\tau>0
:

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0,\delta}\lvert u_{t}^{\delta}(\tau)\rvert^{2}+\int_{0}^{\tau}\widetilde{\mu}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lvert\nabla u_{t}^{\delta}\rvert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+(\widetilde{\mu}+\widetilde{\lambda})\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\dvg u_{t}^{\delta})^{2}=\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0,\delta}\lvert{u_{t}^{\delta}}_{|t=0}\rvert^{2}-\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{t}F^{jk}_{\delta}\partial_{k}({u^{\delta}})^{j}_{t},

and hence:

\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0,\delta}\lvert u_{t}^{\delta}(\tau)\rvert^{2}\leqslant\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0,\delta}\lvert{u_{t}^{\delta}}_{|t=0}\rvert^{2}+C_{*}\int_{0}^{\tau}\lVert\partial_{t}F\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

Using the compact embedding of
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
into
L^{2}_{\text{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and the Aubin-Lions Lemma, we obtain
u^{\delta}_{t}(\tau)\to u_{t}(\tau)
a.e in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Consequently, applying Fatou’s Lemma yields:

\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0}\lvert u_{t}(\tau)\rvert^{2}\leqslant\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0}\lvert{u_{t}}_{|t=0}\rvert^{2}+C_{*}\int_{0}^{\tau}\lVert\partial_{t}F\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

from which we deduce:

\limsup_{\tau\to 0}\lVert\sqrt{\rho_{0}}u_{t}(\tau)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant\lVert\sqrt{\rho_{0}}{u_{t}}_{|t=0}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Since

\lVert\sqrt{\rho_{0}}{u_{t}}_{|t=0}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant\liminf_{\tau\to 0}\lVert\sqrt{\rho_{0}}u_{t}(\tau)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

we infer that
\sqrt{\rho_{0}}u_{t}(\tau)
convergences to
\sqrt{\rho_{0}}{u_{t}}_{|t=0}
strongly in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and consequently we obtain the continuity of
u_{t}
at the initial time given that
\rho_{0}
is bounded away
from vacuum.

This solution is unique: the difference
\delta u
of two solutions
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
of (
3.53
) verifies

\begin{cases}\rho_{0}\delta u_{t}-\widetilde{\mu}\Delta\delta u-(\widetilde{\mu}+\widetilde{\lambda})\nabla\delta u=0,\\
\delta u_{|t=0}=0\end{cases}

and testing the above equation with
\delta
leads to
\delta u=0
.

From (
3.53
), it is obvious to derive the following expression for
\nabla u
:

\nabla u=-\frac{1}{\widetilde{\mu}}(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla\left[\mathcal{P}\left(\rho_{0}u_{t}\right)-\mathcal{P}\dvg F\right]\\
-\dfrac{1}{2\widetilde{\mu}+\widetilde{\lambda}}(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla\left[\mathcal{Q}(\rho_{0}u_{t})-\mathcal{Q}\dvg F\right].

where
\mathcal{P}
is the Leray projector onto the space of divergence-free vector fields and
\mathcal{Q}
is the projector onto the space of curl-free vector fields.
Finally, we can easily show that (
3.11
) also holds for
\nabla u
.
∎

This concludes the proof that
\mathscr{E}
is a nonempty set. The only remaining task is to prove that
\mathscr{E}
is both an open and closed subset of
[0,1]
. This will be addressed in the following step.

3.2.3.
Remainder of the proof of
3.1

We now proceed to the final step in the proof of
3.1
, which is to show that
\mathscr{E}
is both an open and closed subset of
[0,1]
.

Proof.

We begin by
proving that
\mathscr{E}
is an open subset of
[0,1]
. So let
\theta_{0}\in\mathscr{E}
, the goal is to find some
\eta>0
such that

(3.60)

\displaystyle(\theta_{0}-\eta,\theta_{0}+\eta)\cap[0,1]\subset\mathscr{E}.

For this purpose, let
\theta\in[0,1]
,
(F,u_{0})\in Y_{T}^{\theta}
and consider the Cauchy problem (
3.51
) which can be written as:

(3.61)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\rho_{0}u_{t}-\dvg(2\mu^{\theta_{0}}\mathbb{D}u+\lambda^{\theta_{0}}\dvg uI_{d})=\dvg F+(\theta-\theta_{0})\dvg\{2(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}u+(\lambda-\widetilde{\lambda})\dvg uI_{d}\},\\
u_{|t=0}=u_{0}.\end{cases}

We will apply a fixed-point argument to solve the above Cauchy problem with a small
\eta
that does not depend on
F
nor
u_{0}
. In fact, the proof shows that the constant
\eta
only depends on the norm of the viscosity and initial density as required in (
3.2
), as well on the interface characteristics. The solution will be constructed in
X_{T,u_{0}}
which is defined as:

X_{T,u_{0}}:=\{v\in X_{T}\colon v_{|t=0}=u_{0}\},

a complete subset of the Banach space
X_{T}
. So for
v\in X_{T,u_{0}}
, we consider the following equation:

(3.62)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\rho_{0}u_{t}-\dvg(2\mu^{\theta_{0}}\mathbb{D}u+\lambda^{\theta_{0}}\dvg uI_{d})=\dvg F+(\theta-\theta_{0})\dvg\{2(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}v+(\lambda-\widetilde{\lambda})\dvg vI_{d}\},\\
u_{|t=0}=u_{0}.\end{cases}

The regularity of
v
and
\mu,\lambda
ensures that the right hand side written as
\dvg F(v)
with

(3.63)

\displaystyle F(v):=F+(\theta-\theta_{0})\{2(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}v+(\lambda-\widetilde{\lambda})\dvg vI_{d}\}

verifies
(F(v),u_{0})\in Y_{T}^{\theta_{0}}
. The last condition in the definition of
Y_{T}^{\theta_{0}}
(see (
3.3
)) is valid since
v_{|t=0}=u_{0}
. Indeed, we have

\displaystyle\dvg\Big(2\mu^{\theta_{0}}_{0}\mathbb{D}u_{0}

\displaystyle+\lambda^{\theta_{0}}_{0}\dvg u_{0}I_{d}+F(v)_{|t=0}\Big)

\displaystyle=\dvg\Big(2\big(\mu^{\theta_{0}}_{0}+(\theta-\theta_{0})(\mu_{0}-\widetilde{\mu})\big)\mathbb{D}u_{0}+\big(\lambda_{0}^{\theta_{0}}+(\theta-\theta_{0})(\lambda_{0}-\widetilde{\lambda})\big)\dvg u_{0}I_{d}+F_{|t=0}\Big)

(3.64)

\displaystyle=\dvg\Big(2\mu^{\theta}_{0}\mathbb{D}u_{0}+\lambda^{\theta}_{0}\dvg u_{0}I_{d}+F_{|t=0}\Big)\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\;\text{ since }\;(F,u_{0})\in Y^{\theta}_{T}.

Now, owing to the fact that
\theta_{0}\in\mathscr{E}
, the underlying equation admits a unique solution

\Phi(v)\in X_{T,u_{0}}
that verifies all the estimates in
3.1
, where
F
is replaced by
F(v)
.

In the following we are looking for some small
\theta-\theta_{0}
and some
R_{T}
which depends on the initial data such that the condition
\lVert v\rVert_{X_{T}}\leqslant R_{T}

implies the same for
\Phi(v)
. As a standard notation, from now on
A\lesssim B
means
A\leqslant CB
with a constant
C
depending on the bounds of
\mu,\lambda,\rho_{0}
, interface characteristics, and on all of the norms of
\mu,\lambda
as required in (
3.2
).

Step 1: Estimates for
\Phi(v)
and
\partial_{t}\Phi(v)

Since
\Phi(v)
solves the Cauchy problem (
3.62
),
(F(v),u_{0})\in Y_{T}^{\theta_{0}}
, and
\theta_{0}\in\mathscr{E}
,
one has from the a priori estimate the following estimate for
\Phi(v)
:

\displaystyle\sup_{[0,T]}\lVert\Phi(v),\,\partial_{t}\Phi(v),\,\nabla\Phi(v)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla\Phi(v),\,\nabla\partial_{t}\Phi(v),\,\partial_{t}\Phi(v)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\lesssim\lVert u_{0}\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\lVert\partial_{t}\Phi(v)_{|t=0}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

(3.65)

\displaystyle+\sup_{[0,T]}\lVert F(v)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\partial_{t}F(v)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert F(v)\rVert_{L^{2}\cap L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}^{2}.

On the one hand, from (
3.63
), we infer that:

\displaystyle\sup_{[0,T]}\lVert F(v)-F\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert F(v)-F\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lvert\theta-\theta_{0}\rvert^{2}\left\{\sup_{[0,T]}\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\right\}

(3.66)

\displaystyle\lesssim\lvert\theta-\theta_{0}\rvert^{2}\lVert v\rVert_{X_{T}}^{2}

and

\int_{0}^{T}\lVert F(v)-F\rVert_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}^{2}\lesssim\lvert\theta-\theta_{0}\rvert^{2}\lVert v\rVert_{X_{T}}^{2}.

On the other hand, from (
3.63
) one can express the time derivative of
F(v)
as:

(3.67)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}F(v)=F_{t}+(\theta-\theta_{0})\{2(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}v_{t}+(\lambda-\widetilde{\lambda})\dvg v_{t}I_{d}+2\mu_{t}\mathbb{D}v+\lambda_{t}\dvg vI_{d}\}

and consequently, it holds:

(3.68)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\partial_{t}F(v)-F_{t}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\lesssim\lvert\theta-\theta_{0}\rvert^{2}\left\{\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{4}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla v_{t}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\right\}\lesssim\lvert\theta-\theta_{0}\rvert^{2}\lVert v\rVert_{X_{T}}^{2}.

Gathering all of these estimates, one has the following:

(3.69)

\displaystyle\sup_{[0,T]}\lVert\Phi(v),\,\partial_{t}\Phi(v),\,\nabla\Phi(v)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla\Phi(v),\,\nabla\partial_{t}\Phi(v),\,\partial_{t}\Phi(v)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\lesssim\lVert(F,u_{0})\rVert_{Y_{T}^{\theta}}^{2}+\lvert\theta-\theta_{0}\rvert^{2}\lVert v\rVert_{X_{T}}^{2}.

Also, one has the following estimate for
\nabla\Phi(v)
:

(3.70)

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla\Phi(v)\rVert_{L^{\infty}((0,T),L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\lesssim\lVert F(v)\rVert_{L^{\infty}((0,T),L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}+\sup_{[0,T]}\lVert F(v),\partial_{t}\Phi(v),\nabla\Phi(v)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Owing to the fact that the estimate (
3.69
) provides a bound for
\partial_{t}\Phi(v)
in
L^{\infty}((0,T),L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
and for
\Phi(v)
in
L^{\infty}((0,T),H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
and owing to the estimate (
3.66
), we only need to estimate the
L^{\infty}((0,T),L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
norm of
F(v)
. In fact, this can be easily obtained from (
3.63
) as:

\lVert F(v)-F\rVert_{L^{\infty}((0,T),L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\lesssim\lvert\theta-\theta_{0}\rvert\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{\infty}((0,T),L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\lesssim\lvert\theta-\theta_{0}\rvert\lVert v\rVert_{X_{T}}.

Gathering all of theses estimates, one has:

(3.71)

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla\Phi(v)\rVert_{L^{\infty}((0,T),L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\lesssim\lVert(F,u_{0})\rVert_{Y_{T}^{\theta}}+\lvert\theta-\theta_{0}\rvert\lVert v\rVert_{X_{T}}.

We turn to the
L^{16}((0,T),L^{8}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
estimate for
\nabla\Phi(v)
. For this purpose, we recall from (
3.8
) that:

\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla\Phi(v)\rVert_{L^{8}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{16}\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\lVert F(v)\rVert_{L^{8}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{16}+\lVert F(v),\partial_{t}\Phi(v),\nabla\Phi(v)\rVert_{L^{2}\cap L^{\infty}((0,T),L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}^{16}+\lVert\nabla\partial_{t}\Phi(v)\rVert_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{16}.

We have already obtained bound for the last two terms above in (
3.69
) and (
3.66
) and we have only to estimate the first one. From (
3.63
), we have:

\int_{0}^{T}\lVert F(v)-F\rVert_{L^{8}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}^{16}\lesssim\lvert\theta-\theta_{0}\rvert^{16}\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{8}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}^{16}\lesssim\lvert\theta-\theta_{0}\rvert^{p}\lVert v\rVert_{X_{T}}^{16}

and hence:

(3.72)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla\Phi(v)\rVert_{L^{8}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{16}\lesssim\lVert(F,u_{0})\rVert_{Y_{T}^{\theta}}^{p}+\lvert\theta-\theta_{0}\rvert^{16}\lVert v\rVert_{X_{T}}^{16}.

Step 2: Estimates for
\partial_{tt}\Phi(v)
and
L^{4}
-norm estimate for
\nabla\partial_{t}\Phi(v)

As in the previous step, one first recalls that
\Phi(v)
verifies:

\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{tt}\Phi(v),\;\sigma\nabla\partial_{tt}\Phi(v)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\lVert\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{t}\nabla\Phi(v),\;\sigma\partial_{tt}\Phi(v)\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}((0,T),L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\\
\lesssim\int_{0}^{\sigma(T)}\lVert\partial_{t}\nabla\Phi(v)\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\partial_{t}F(v),\,\sigma\partial_{tt}F(v)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\lVert\nabla\Phi(v)\rVert_{L^{\infty}((0,T),L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}^{2}.

All terms that appear in the right hand side above are controlled in (
3.69
), (
3.71
), (
3.66
) except the
L^{2}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
norm of
\sigma\partial_{tt}F(v)
which is:

\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{2}\lVert\partial_{tt}F(v)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\leqslant\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{2}\lVert F_{tt}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{2}\lVert\partial_{tt}F(v)-F_{tt}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

In order to link the last term to the norm of
v
, we first express
\partial_{tt}F(v)-F_{tt}
, from (
3.67
) as

\partial_{tt}F(v)-F_{tt}=(\theta-\theta_{0})\{2(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}v_{tt}+(\lambda-\widetilde{\lambda})\dvg v_{tt}I_{d}+4\mu_{t}\mathbb{D}v_{t}+2\lambda_{t}\dvg v_{t}I_{d}+2\mu_{tt}\mathbb{D}v+\lambda_{tt}\dvg vI_{d}\}

and then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{2}\lVert\partial_{tt}F(v)-F_{tt}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lvert\theta-\theta_{0}\rvert^{2}\lVert v\rVert_{X_{T}}^{2}

from which, we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{tt}\Phi(v),\;\sigma\nabla\partial_{tt}\Phi(v)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

\displaystyle+\lVert\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{t}\nabla\Phi(v),\;\sigma\partial_{tt}\Phi(v)\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}((0,T),L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}

(3.73)

\displaystyle\lesssim\lVert(F,u_{0})\rVert_{Y_{T}^{\theta}}^{2}+\lvert\theta-\theta_{0}\rvert^{2}\lVert v\rVert_{X_{T}}^{2}.

This ends the computations of the higher-order Hoff estimates. Now, we may turn to the
L^{8/d}((0,T),L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
norm estimate for
\sigma^{\tfrac{d}{4}}\nabla\partial_{t}\Phi(v)
. Thanks to
(
3.10
), Hölder’s and Young’s inequality, we have:

\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{2}\lVert\nabla\partial_{t}\Phi(v)\rVert_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{8/d}\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{2}\lVert\partial_{t}F(v)\rVert_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{8/d}+\left[\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla\Phi(v)\rVert_{L^{8}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{16}\right]^{1/(2d)}+\lVert\sigma\partial_{tt}\Phi(v)\rVert_{L^{\infty}((0,T),L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}^{8/d}\\
+\lVert\nabla\Phi(v)\rVert_{L^{4}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{8/d}+\left[\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla\partial_{t}\Phi(v),\,\partial_{t}F(v)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\right]^{4/d}.

The estimates (
3.2.3
) and (
3.72
), (
3.71
) and (
3.68
) control all of the terms that appear in the left hand side above, except the
L^{8/d}((0,T),L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
-norm of
\sigma^{\tfrac{d}{4}}\partial_{t}F(v)
. Starting from (
3.67
), one has:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{2}\lVert\partial_{t}F(v)-F_{t}\rVert_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{8/d}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lvert\theta-\theta_{0}\rvert^{8/d}\left\{\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{2}\lVert\nabla v_{t}\rVert_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{8/d}+\left[\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{8}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{16}\right]^{1/(2d)}\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lvert\theta-\theta_{0}\rvert^{8/d}\lVert v\rVert_{X_{T}}^{8/d}.

Gathering all of the estimates one obtains the following:

\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{2}\lVert\partial_{t}\nabla\Phi(v)\rVert_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{8/d}\lesssim\lVert(F,u_{0})\rVert_{Y_{T}^{\theta}}^{8/d}+\lvert\theta-\theta_{0}\rvert^{8/d}\lVert v\rVert_{X_{T}}^{8/d}.

We now turn to the last step:

Step 3: Piecewise Hölder-norm estimate for
\nabla\Phi(v)

As above, thanks to (
3.11
), the following Hölder estimate holds for
\nabla\Phi(v)
:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{r}\lVert\nabla\Phi(v)\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{r}\lVert F(v)\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{4}+\left[\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{4/d}\lVert\partial_{t}\nabla\Phi(v)\rVert_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{8/d}\right]^{d/2}

\displaystyle+\lVert F(v),\,\nabla\Phi(v),\,\partial_{t}\Phi(v),\,\sqrt{\sigma}\nabla\partial_{t}\Phi(v)\rVert_{L^{\infty}((0,T),L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}^{4}

\displaystyle+\left[\int_{0}^{T}\lVert F(v),\,\nabla\Phi(v),\,\partial_{t}\Phi(v),\,\partial_{t}\nabla\Phi(v)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\right]^{2}.

According to the above computations, one only needs to estimate the first term of the right hand side above and from (
3.63
) one has:

\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{r}\lVert F(v)-F\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{4}\lesssim\lvert\theta-\theta_{0}\rvert^{4}\lVert v\rVert_{X_{T}}^{4}.

Gathering all of these estimates, one has the following:

(3.74)

\displaystyle\lVert\Phi(v)\rVert_{X_{T}}\leqslant C\left(R_{0}+\lvert\theta-\theta_{0}\rvert\lVert v\rVert_{X_{T}}\right)

where
R_{0}
depends on the norm of
(F,u_{0})\in Y_{T}^{\theta}
and the constant
C
that depends not only on the bounds of the density and viscosity, on the interface characteristics, and all of the norm of the viscosity
\mu,\,\lambda
and initial density
\rho_{0}
as required in (
3.2
). So, choosing large
R_{T}
, for instance
R_{T}=2CR_{0}
and
\lvert\theta-\theta_{0}\rvert\leqslant\min\{1,1/(2C)\}
, one obtains
\lVert\Phi(v)\rVert_{X_{T}}\leqslant R_{T}
as soon as
\lVert v\rVert_{X_{T}}\leqslant R_{T}
. We thus define a map
\Phi
from the close ball
B_{X_{T,u_{0}}}(0,R_{T})
of
X_{T,u_{0}}
to itself.

Step 4: Construction of the approximate sequence and proof of its convergence

We construct the sequence of approximate solutions
\big(u^{n}\big)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
, inductively, as follows:

\displaystyle u_{n+1}=\Phi(u_{n}),\;n\geqslant 0,

with

(3.75)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\rho_{0}\partial_{t}u_{n+1}-\dvg(2\mu^{\theta_{0}}\mathbb{D}u_{n+1}+\lambda^{\theta_{0}}\dvg u_{n+1}I_{d})&=\dvg F+(\theta-\theta_{0})\dvg\big(2(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}u_{n}\\
&+(\lambda-\widetilde{\lambda})\dvg u_{n}I_{d}\big),\\
{u_{n+1}}_{|t=0}&=u_{0}.\end{cases}

It follows from the conclusion of Step 3 that the sequence
(u_{n})_{n}
is uniformly bounded in
X_{T}
:

(3.76)

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\lVert u_{n}\rVert_{X_{T}}\leqslant R_{T}.

In the following lines, we will prove that
(u_{n})_{n}
converges strongly in
L^{\infty}((0,T),L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))\cap L^{2}((0,T),H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
to some
u\in X_{T,u_{0}}
which satisfies (
3.61
).

To achieve this, we take the differences in (
3.75
) and obtain:

\begin{cases}\rho_{0}\partial_{t}w_{n+1}-\dvg\Big(2\mu^{\theta_{0}}\mathbb{D}(w_{n+1})+\lambda^{\theta_{0}}\dvg(w_{n+1})I_{d}\Big)=(\theta-\theta_{0})\dvg\Big(2(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}(w_{n})+(\lambda-\widetilde{\lambda})\dvg(w_{n})I_{d}\Big),\\
{w_{n+1}}_{|t=0}=0,\end{cases}

where
w_{k+1}=u_{k+1}-u_{k}
, with
k\geqslant 1
. By multiplying the equation above by
w_{n+1}
, integrating in space, and applying Korn’s inequality (see (
3.13
)), along with Young’s and Hölder’s inequalities, we obtain:

\dfrac{1}{2}\dfrac{d}{dt}\lVert\sqrt{\rho}_{0}w_{n+1}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\dfrac{\mu_{*}}{2}\lVert\nabla w_{n+1}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\leqslant C\lvert\theta-\theta_{0}\rvert^{2}\lVert\nabla w_{n}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

Time integration, along with the condition
{w_{n+1}}_{|t=0}=0
yields:

\sup_{[0,T]}\lVert w_{n+1}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla w_{n+1}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\leqslant C\lvert\theta-\theta_{0}\rvert^{2}\Bigg(\sup_{[0,T]}\lVert w_{n}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla w_{n}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Bigg),

and therefore:

\displaystyle\sup_{[0,T]}\lVert u_{n+1}-u_{n}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla\big(u_{n+1}-u_{n}\big)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C^{n}\lvert\theta-\theta_{0}\rvert^{2n}\Bigg(\sup_{[0,T]}\lVert w_{1}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla w_{1}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leqslant 2C^{n}\lvert\theta-\theta_{0}\rvert^{2n}R_{T}^{2}.

By choosing

(3.77)

\displaystyle\lvert\theta-\theta_{0}\rvert^{2}<\frac{1}{2C},

we end up with the strong convergence of
(u_{n})_{n}
to a limit
u
in
L^{\infty}((0,T),L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))\cap L^{2}((0,T),H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
.
It is a simple matter to check from (
3.75
) that
u
is a weak solution of

(3.78)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\rho_{0}\partial_{t}u-\dvg(2\mu^{\theta_{0}}\mathbb{D}u+\lambda^{\theta_{0}}\dvg uI_{d})=\dvg F+(\theta-\theta_{0})\dvg\big(2(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}u+(\lambda-\widetilde{\lambda})\dvg uI_{d}\big),\\
u_{|t=0}=u_{0},\end{cases}

and consequently,
of (
3.51
). Then, by virtue of lower semi-continuity of norms,
we deduce form the uniform bound (
3.76
):

\lVert w\rVert_{X_{T}}\leqslant R_{T}.

Thus
u
has all the regularity required in (
3.4
), and it only remains to establish time continuity:

u\in\mathscr{C}([0,T],H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})),\,\text{ and }\,\partial_{t}u\in\mathscr{C}([0,T],L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})).

The first one follows from the fact that
\partial_{t}u\in L^{2}((0,T),H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
. Moreover, since
\sqrt{\sigma}u_{tt}\in L^{2}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
, it transpires that
u_{t}\in\mathscr{C}((0,T],L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
. Therefore, it only remains to prove the continuity of
u_{t}
at the initial time. To achieve this, we observe that the sequence
(u_{n})_{n}
satisfies the following estimate (see (
3.17
)):

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0}\lvert\partial_{t}u_{n}(\tau)\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0}\lvert{\partial_{t}u_{n}}_{|t=0}\rvert^{2}+C\Bigg(\int_{0}^{\tau}\lVert F_{t}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{\tau}\lVert\mu_{t}^{\theta},\,\lambda_{t}^{\theta}\rVert_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\lVert\nabla u_{n}\rVert_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leqslant\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0}\lvert{\partial_{t}u_{n}}_{|t=0}\rvert^{2}+\tau CR_{T}^{2}+C\int_{0}^{\tau}\lVert F_{t}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

For all
n\geqslant 1
, since
{u_{n+1}}_{|t=0}={u_{n}}_{|t=0}=u_{0}
, it follows from (
3.78
)-(
3.64
) that:

\rho_{0}{\partial_{t}u_{n+1}}_{|t=0}=\dvg\Big(2\mu_{0}^{\theta}\mathbb{D}u_{0}+\lambda_{0}^{\theta}\dvg u_{0}+F_{|t=0}\Big)={\rho_{0}u_{t}}_{|t=0}.

Next, using the compact embedding
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
into
L^{2}_{\text{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and Aubins-Lions theorem, we obtain
w^{n}_{t}(\tau)\to u_{t}(\tau)
a.e in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Therefore, Fatou’s Lemma yields:

\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0}\lvert u_{t}(\tau)\rvert^{2}\leqslant\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0}\lvert{u_{t}}_{|t=0}\rvert^{2}+\tau CR_{T}^{2}+C\int_{0}^{\tau}\lVert F_{t}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

from which we deduce

\limsup_{\tau\to 0}\lVert\sqrt{\rho_{0}}u_{t}(\tau)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant\lVert\sqrt{\rho_{0}}{u_{t}}_{|t=0}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

This yields the continuity of
u_{t}
at the initial time, and thereby completing the proof that
u\in X_{T,u_{0}}
.

Given that the constant
C
appearing in (
3.74
) and (
3.77
) does not depend on
u_{0}
nor
F
, it makes the condition (
3.60
) true, for all
(F,u_{0})\in Y_{T}^{\theta}

and one deduces that
\mathscr{E}
is an open set of
[0,1]
.

We turn to the proof of the fact that
\mathscr{E}
is a closed set of
[0,1]
.
Let
(\theta_{n})_{n}
a sequence of element of
\mathscr{E}
that converges to some
\theta_{0}\in[0,1]
. Our aim is to prove that
\theta_{0}\in\mathscr{E}
. In other words, the following Cauchy problem

(3.79)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\rho_{0}u_{t}-\dvg(2\mu^{\theta_{n}}\mathbb{D}u+\lambda^{\theta_{n}}\dvg uI_{d})=\dvg F+(\theta_{0}-\theta_{n})\dvg\{2(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}u+(\lambda-\widetilde{\lambda})\dvg uI_{d}\},\\
u_{|t=0}=u_{0}\end{cases}

admits a unique solution in
X_{T}
for all
(F,u_{0})\in Y_{T}^{\theta_{0}}
.
By following the computations in Step 1-Step 4, and given that the norms of
\mu^{\theta}
and
\lambda^{\theta}
are uniformly bounded with respect to
\theta
, we can choose a sufficiently large
n
independently of
F
,
u_{0}
. Hereby,
\mathscr{E}
is a closed set of
[0,1]
.

In conclusion,
\mathscr{E}
is at same time a non-empty, open and closed set of
[0,1]
, since
[0,1]
is a connected set,
\mathscr{E}=[0,1]
,
1\in\mathscr{E}
and
3.1
finally holds.
∎

4.
Study of the full nonlinear system (
2.8
)

4.1.
Preliminary

This section is devoted to the solvability of the full nonlinear two-fluid model in Lagrangian coordinates (
2.8
). The initial data
(c_{0},\rho_{0},u_{0})
is such that the compatibility condition holds true, that is:

(4.1)

\displaystyle\dvg(2\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})\mathbb{D}u_{0}+(\lambda(\rho_{0},c_{0})\dvg u_{0}-P(\rho_{0},c_{0}))I_{d})\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Our method relies on a fixed-point argument, thus we will begin by setting some tools. We recall that such solution
u
verifies (for simplicity, we write
u
instead of
\overline{u}
):

(4.2)

\displaystyle\rho_{0}u_{t}-\dvg\{2\mu(J^{-1}_{u}\rho_{0},c_{0})\mathbb{D}u+\lambda(J^{-1}_{u}\rho_{0},c_{0}))\dvg uI_{d}\}=\dvg I(u,u)

where

I(u,u)=I_{1}(u,u)+I_{2}(u,u)+I_{3}(u)

and

(4.3)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}I_{1}(v,w)&=2\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\{\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\mathbb{D}_{A_{v}}w-\mathbb{D}w\},\\
I_{2}(v,w)&=\lambda(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\{\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\dvg_{A_{v}}w-\dvg w\},\\
I_{3}(v)&=\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\{P(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{P}\}I_{d}.\end{cases}

According to the previous section, we will construct
u
in a closed subset of the vector space
X_{T}
as defined
in (
3.4
)
for some small time
T
. For some
v\in X_{T}
with
v_{|t=0}=u_{0}
, we consider the linear Cauchy problem:

(4.4)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\rho_{0}u_{t}-\dvg\{2\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\mathbb{D}u+\lambda(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\dvg\ uId\}=\dvg I(v,v),\\
u_{|t=0}=u_{0}.\end{cases}

The validity of the last condition in the definition of
Y_{T}:=Y_{T}(\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0}),\lambda(\rho_{0},c_{0}))
(see (
3.3
)) results from the fact that
v_{|t=0}=u_{0}
,
{\big(J_{v}^{-1}\big)}_{|t=0}=1
,
I(v,v)_{|t=0}=\big(P(\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{P}\big)I_{d}
and (
4.1
). In the following section, we set up tools for the construction of the solution to (
4.2
).

4.2.
Implementation of the fixed-point theorem

In this section, we will justify the well-posedness of a contracting map whose fixed-point is the solution of the nonlinear system (
4.2
). To this end, we fix some
v
in
X_{T,u_{0}}=\{u\in X_{T}\colon u_{|t=0}=u_{0}\}
. The first part of the following section will consist in proving that the regularity of
v\in X_{T}
is sufficient to ensure that
I(v,v)
belongs to
Y_{T}
. From there, we will be able to deduce the existence of a solution for the Cauchy problem (
4.4
) using
3.1
. This leads to the following result.

Proposition 4.1
.

There are
T^{*}>0
and
R>0
such that for all
T\in(0,T_{*})
the map:

\displaystyle\Phi_{T}\colon\;X_{T,u_{0}}(R)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow X_{T,u_{0}}(R)

(4.5)

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle\longmapsto\Phi_{T}(v):=u,

where
u
is the unique solution of the Cauchy problem (
4.4
), and
X_{T,u_{0}}(R):=\{u\in X_{T,u_{0}}\colon\lVert u\rVert_{X_{T}}\leqslant R\}
, is well-defined.

The final step of this section is dedicated to proving that for some small
T\in(0,T_{*})
, the map
\Phi_{T}
admits a fixed point, see
4.2
below.

Proof of
4.1
.

To ease readability, for
T>0
, and a normed space
\mathcal{X}
, the space
L^{p}((0,T),\mathcal{X})
will be denoted
L^{p}_{T}\mathcal{X}
. Also, we will only present the main steps, technical computations are carried out in
section

4.3
below.

First of all, let us point out that since
\sigma^{r/4}\nabla v\in L^{4}_{T}\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, with
0<r<8/3
, one has:

c=\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant T^{\tfrac{3-r}{4}}\lVert v\rVert_{X_{T}}.

Suppose that
\lVert v\rVert_{X_{T}}\leqslant R
for some
R>0
to be fixed later. One chooses
T\leqslant 1
small in such a way that
2c<1
. Thus, the
constants involved in
4.4
and
4.5
below are assumed to be fixed. Furthermore, the constant in the notation
\lesssim
in all subsequent steps, may depend on
c<1/2
, the bounds of
\rho_{0},\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0}),\lambda(\rho_{0},c_{0})
, the piecewise Hölder norm of
\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})
,
\lambda(\rho_{0},c_{0})
and the interface regularity.

The following bounds for
I(v,v)
will be proved in
section

4.3.1
(we freely use
T\leqslant 1
):

(4.6)

\displaystyle\sup_{[0,T]}\lVert I(v,v)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\lVert I(v,v)\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\lesssim\lVert P(\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{P}\rVert_{L^{2}\cap L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+T^{\tfrac{3-r}{4}}R(1+R),

(4.7)

\displaystyle\lVert I(v,v)\rVert_{L^{16}_{T}L^{8}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim T^{1/16}\lVert P(\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{P}\rVert_{L^{8}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+T^{\tfrac{3-r}{4}}R(1+R),

(4.8)

\displaystyle\lVert\sigma^{r/4}I(v,v)\rVert_{L^{4}_{T}\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\lesssim T^{1/4}\lVert P(\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{P}\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+T^{\tfrac{3-r}{4}}R(1+R).

On the other hand, the following estimates for
\partial_{t}I(v,v)
will be proved in
section

4.3.2
(we freely use
T^{\tfrac{3-r}{4}}R\leqslant 1
):

(4.9)

\displaystyle\lVert\partial_{t}I(v,v)\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim T^{1/2}R(R+1)+T^{\tfrac{3-r}{4}}R^{2},

(4.10)

\displaystyle\lVert\sigma^{\tfrac{d}{4}}\partial_{t}I(v,v)\rVert_{L^{8/d}_{T}L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim T^{\tfrac{d}{8}}R(R+1)+T^{\tfrac{3-r}{4}}R^{2},

and finally, we will derive in
section

4.3.3
the following estimates for
\partial_{tt}I(v,v)
:

(4.11)

\displaystyle\lVert\sigma\partial_{tt}I(v,v)\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim T^{1/2}R(R+1)+T^{\tfrac{1}{2}-\tfrac{d}{8}}R^{2}(1+T^{\tfrac{d}{8}}R)+T^{\tfrac{3-r}{4}}R^{2}.

We obtain that
I(v,v)
satisfies all conditions required in the definition of
Y_{T}
. Now, we shall ensure that the viscosity
\mu(J_{v}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})
and
\lambda(J_{v}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})
have the regularities required in (
3.2
), and the dynamic viscosity
\mu(J_{v}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})
remains
close to
\widetilde{\mu}
. We prove in
section

4.3.4
:

(4.12)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\lVert\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{\mu}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{T}\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}&\leqslant\lVert\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{\mu}\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\lVert\partial_{\rho}\mu\rVert_{L^{\infty}}T^{\tfrac{3-r}{4}}R,\\
\lVert\llbracket\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}&\leqslant\lVert\llbracket\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}+\lVert\partial_{\rho}\mu\rVert_{L^{\infty}}T^{\tfrac{3-r}{4}}R,\end{cases}

with similar bounds for
\lambda(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})
. Assuming (
1.14
)-(
1.15
), we choose
T
small such that

\displaystyle\sup_{[0,T]}\left[1\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\lVert\lambda(J_{v}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\left(\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}+\ell^{-\alpha}_{\varphi_{0}}\right)\llbracket\lambda(J_{v}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})\rrbracket_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}\right]\sup_{[0,T]}\lVert\mu(J_{v}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{\mu}\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle+\left(\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}+\ell^{-\alpha}_{\varphi_{0}}\right)\sup_{[0,T]}\lVert\llbracket\mu(J_{v}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}

\displaystyle+\sup_{[0,T]}\left[\lVert\llbracket\mu(J_{v}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})\rrbracket,\,\llbracket\lambda(J_{v}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}\left\|1-\dfrac{\widetilde{\mu}}{\langle\mu(J_{v}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})\rangle}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{0})}\right]\leqslant\dfrac{[\mu]}{2},

and

\mu_{*,0}/4\leqslant\mu(J_{v}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})\quad\text{and}\quad\lambda(J_{v}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0}),\;\mu(J_{v}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})\leqslant 4\max\left\{\sup_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0}),\;\sup_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lambda(\rho_{0},c_{0})\right\}.

We prove in
section

4.3.5
below, the following estimates for the time derivative of the viscosity:

(4.13)

\displaystyle\lVert\partial_{t}\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0}),\,\partial_{t}\lambda(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim R,\quad\quad\;\lVert\partial_{t}\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0}),\,\partial_{t}\lambda(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\rVert_{L^{16}_{T}L^{8}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim R,

and

(4.14)

\displaystyle\lVert\partial_{t}\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0}),\,\partial_{t}\lambda(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\rVert_{L^{1}_{T}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\lVert\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{t}\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0}),\,\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{t}\lambda(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim T^{\tfrac{3-r}{4}}R.

Finally, the
L^{2}_{T}L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-norm of the second time derivative of the viscosity is obtained in
section

4.3.6
, from which we deduce:

(4.15)

\displaystyle\lVert\sigma\partial_{tt}\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0}),\,\sigma\partial_{tt}\lambda(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim T^{\tfrac{1}{2}-\tfrac{d}{8}}R(1+R).

Hence, the viscosities
\mu
and
\lambda
verify all the conditions stated in (
3.2
) and in particular
3.1
provides us with a unique solution

u\in X_{T,u_{0}}
of the Cauchy problem (
4.4
).

Estimates for
u
and
u_{t}

\displaystyle\sup_{[0,T]}\lVert u,\,u_{t},\,\nabla u\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

\displaystyle+\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{L^{16}_{T}L^{8}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla u,\,\nabla u_{t},\,u_{t}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

(4.16)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\lVert(I(v,v),u_{0})\rVert_{Y_{T}}^{2}+TR^{2}\lVert I(v,v)\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{T}(L^{2}\cap L^{6})(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\right)\exp\left(C_{*}T^{\tfrac{3-r}{4}}R+C_{*}TR^{2}\right)

with the estimates of
I(v,v)
given in (
4.6
)-(
4.11
).

Estimates for
u_{tt}

(4.17)

\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\sqrt{\sigma}u_{tt},\;\sigma\nabla u_{tt}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\lVert\sqrt{\sigma}\nabla u_{t},\;\sigma u_{tt}\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\left[\int_{0}^{\sigma(T)}\lVert\nabla u_{t}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right.\\
\left.+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\partial_{t}I(v,v),\,\sigma\partial_{tt}I(v,v)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+(TR^{2}+T^{1-\tfrac{d}{4}}R^{2}(1+R)^{2})\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\right]\times\exp\left(C_{*}T^{\tfrac{3-r}{4}}R\right).

The norms of
u
involved in this estimate are controlled in (
4.16
), while those of
I(v,v)
are given in (
4.6
)-(
4.11
).

L^{8/d}_{T}L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-norm estimate for
\sqrt{\sigma}\nabla u_{t}

(4.18)

\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{2}\lVert\nabla u_{t}\rVert_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{8/d}\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{2}\lVert\partial_{t}I(v,v)\rVert_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{8/d}+T^{1-\tfrac{1}{d}}R^{8/d}\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{L^{16}_{T}L^{8}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{8/d}+\lVert\sigma u_{tt}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{8/d}\\
+\left[\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla u_{t},\,\partial_{t}I(v,v)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\right]^{4/d}+T^{4/d}R^{8/d}\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{8/d}.

The norms of
I(v,v)
are given in (
4.10
) and (
4.9
), while the norms of
u
are controlled in (
4.16
) and (
4.17
).

L^{4}_{T}\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
estimate for
\sigma^{r/4}\nabla u

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{r}\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{r}\lVert I(v,v)\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{4}+\lVert I(v,v),\,\nabla u,\,u_{t},\,\sqrt{\sigma}\nabla u_{t}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{4}

\displaystyle+\left[\int_{0}^{T}\lVert I(v,v),\,\nabla u,\,u_{t},\,\nabla u_{t}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\right]^{2}+\left[\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{2}\lVert\nabla u_{t}\rVert_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{8/d}\right]^{d/2}.

The norms of
I(v,v)
are given in (
4.6
) and (
4.8
) whereas the norms of
u
involved are controlled in (
4.16
), (
4.17
) and (
4.18
).

By combining all these computations, we choose
R
related to the norm of the initial data and a small
T^{*}
such that for all
0<T\leqslant T_{*}
,

\lVert u\rVert_{X_{T}}\leqslant R.

This concludes the proof of
4.1
.
∎

The final step of this section is dedicated to constructing the fixed point
u\in X_{T,u_{0}}
of
\Phi_{T}
.

Proposition 4.2
.

There exists
T\in(0,T_{*})
and
u\in X_{T,u_{0}}
such that

u=\Phi_{T}(u).

This fixed point
u
satisfies the nonlinear equations (
4.2
), thereby completing the proof of
2.2
.

Proof of
4.2
.

To prove
4.2
, we begin by considering the following sequence:

\displaystyle u_{n+1}=\Phi_{T}(u_{n}),\;n\geqslant 0,

with

(4.19)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\rho_{0}\partial_{t}u_{n+1}-\dvg\{2\mu(J^{-1}_{u_{n}}\rho_{0},c_{0})\mathbb{D}u_{n+1}+\lambda(J^{-1}_{u_{n}}\rho_{0},c_{0})\dvg u_{n+1}I_{d}\}=\dvg\big(I(u_{n},u_{n})\big),\\
{u_{n+1}}_{|t=0}=u_{0}.\end{cases}

From
4.1
, it follows that the sequence
(u_{n})_{n}
is uniformly bounded in
X_{T}
:

(4.20)

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\lVert u_{n}\rVert_{X_{T}}\leqslant R.

We take the differences in (
4.19
) and obtain:

(4.21)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\rho_{0}\partial_{t}w_{n+1}&-\dvg\Big(2\mu(J_{u_{n}}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})\mathbb{D}w_{n+1}+\lambda(J_{u_{n}}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})\dvg w_{n+1}I_{d}\Big)\\
&=\dvg\big(\delta\mathcal{I}\big)+\dvg\big(I(u_{n},u_{n})-I(u_{n-1},u_{n-1})\big),\\
{w_{n+1}}_{|t=0}&=0,\end{cases}

where
w_{k+1}=u_{k+1}-u_{k}
,
k\geqslant 1
, and

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{I}=2\big((\mu(J^{-1}_{u_{n}}\rho_{0},c_{0})-\mu(J^{-1}_{u_{n-1}}\rho_{0},c_{0})\big)\mathbb{D}u_{n-1}+\big(\lambda(J^{-1}_{u_{n}}\rho_{0},c_{0})-\lambda(J^{-1}_{u_{n-1}}\rho_{0},c_{0})\big)\dvg u_{n-1}I_{d}.

An energy estimate in (
4.21
) yields:

(4.22)

\displaystyle\sup_{[0,T]}\lVert w_{n+1}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla w_{n+1}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\left\lVert\delta\mathcal{I},\,I(u_{n},u_{n})-I(u_{n-1},u_{n-1})\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

To estimate the right-hand side above in terms of the norms of
u_{n}
and
u_{n-1}
, we rely on the following result from
[
5
, Lemma A.4]
.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
v_{1},\,v_{2}
be two vectors fields verifying

c=\max_{j}\int_{0}^{T}\lVert Dv_{j}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}<1.

There exists a constant
k=k(c)
such that the following estimates hold true: for all
p\in[2,\infty]

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\lVert A_{v_{2}}(t)-A_{v_{1}}(t)\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}&\leqslant k\lVert D(v_{2}-v_{1})\rVert_{L^{1}_{t}L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\\
