\lVert\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v_{2}}(t))-\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v_{1}}(t))\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}&\leqslant k\lVert D(v_{2}-v_{1})\rVert_{L^{1}_{t}L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\\
\lVert J^{\pm 1}_{v_{2}}(t)-J^{\pm 1}_{v_{1}}(t)\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}&\leqslant k\lVert D(v_{2}-v_{1})\rVert_{L^{1}_{t}L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.\end{cases}

4.3
immediately implies:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\delta\mathcal{I}(t)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}dt

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\lVert J_{u_{n}}^{-1}(t)-J_{u_{n-1}}^{-1}(t)\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lVert Du_{n-1}(t)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}dt

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}t\lVert Du_{n-1}(t)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}dt\bigg)\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\lVert Dw_{n}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{3-r}{2}}R^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\lVert Dw_{n}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

Similarly, by expressing (see the definitions of
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
in (
4.3
))

\displaystyle I_{3}\big(u_{n}\big)-I_{3}(u_{n-1}\big)

\displaystyle=\{\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{u_{n}})-\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{u_{n-1}})\}\left(P(J_{u_{n}}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{P}\right)

\displaystyle+\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{u_{n-1}})\{P(J_{u_{n}}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})-P(J_{u_{n-1}}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})\},

and

\displaystyle I_{1}\big(u_{n},u_{n}\big)-I_{1}\big(u_{n-1},u_{n-1}\big)

\displaystyle=2\{\mu(J^{-1}_{u_{n}}\rho_{0},c_{0})-\mu(J^{-1}_{u_{n-1}}\rho_{0},c_{0})\}\{\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{u_{n}})\mathbb{D}_{A_{u_{n}}}{u_{n}}-\mathbb{D}{u_{n}}\}

\displaystyle+2\mu(J^{-1}_{u_{n-1}}\rho_{0},c_{0})\{\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{u_{n}})\mathbb{D}_{A_{u_{n}}}w_{n}-\mathbb{D}w_{n}\}

\displaystyle+2\mu(J^{-1}_{u_{n-1}}\rho_{0},c_{0})\{\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{u_{n}})-\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{u_{n-1}})\}\mathbb{D}_{A_{u_{n}}}u_{n-1}

\displaystyle+2\mu(J^{-1}_{u_{n-1}}\rho_{0},c_{0})\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{u_{n-1}})\{\mathbb{D}_{A_{u_{n}}}-\mathbb{D}_{A_{u_{n-1}}}\}u_{n-1},

we infer

\int_{0}^{T}\lVert I_{3}\big(u_{n}\big)-I_{3}\big(u_{n-1}\big)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\lesssim T^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla w_{n}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

and

\int_{0}^{T}\lVert I_{1}\big(u_{n},u_{n}\big)-I_{1}\big(u_{n-1},u_{n-1}\big)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\lesssim T^{\frac{3-r}{2}}R^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla w_{n}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

A similar estimate also holds for
I_{2}\big(u_{n},u_{n}\big)-I_{2}\big(u_{n-1},u_{n-1}\big)
. It therefore follows from (
4.22
) that:

\displaystyle\sup_{[0,T]}\lVert w_{n+1}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla w_{n+1}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\leqslant CT^{\frac{3-r}{2}}\big(1+R^{2}\big)\Bigg(\sup_{[0,T]}\lVert w_{n}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla w_{n}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Bigg).

We then choose
T<T_{*}
small such that

CT^{\frac{3-r}{2}}\big(1+R^{2}\big)\leqslant\dfrac{1}{2},

which leads to

\sup_{[0,T]}\lVert w_{n+1}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla w_{n+1}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\leqslant\dfrac{1}{2}\Bigg(\sup_{[0,T]}\lVert w_{n}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla w_{n}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Bigg),

and hence

\displaystyle\sup_{[0,T]}\lVert w_{n+1}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla w_{n+1}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\dfrac{1}{2^{n}}\Bigg(\sup_{[0,T]}\lVert w_{1}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla\big(w_{1}\big)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leqslant\dfrac{R^{2}}{2^{n-1}}.

This implies the strong convergence of the sequence
(u_{n})_{n}
in
L^{\infty}((0,T),L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))\cap L^{2}((0,T),H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
to some limit
u
. Furthermore, by virtue of lower semi-continuity of norms, we deduce from the uniform bound (
4.20
):

\lVert u\rVert_{X_{T}}\leqslant R.

This enables us to apply
4.3
with
v_{1}=u_{n}
and
v_{2}=u
:

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\lVert A_{u_{n}}-A_{u}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}&\leqslant k\lVert D(u_{n}-u)\rVert_{L^{1}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\\
\lVert\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{u_{n}})-\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{u})\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}&\leqslant k\lVert D(u_{n}-u)\rVert_{L^{1}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\\
\lVert J^{\pm 1}_{u_{n}}-J^{\pm 1}_{u}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}&\leqslant k\lVert D(u_{n}-u)\rVert_{L^{1}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\end{cases}

from which we deduce the strong convergence in
L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
of the sequences
\big(I_{d}-A_{u_{n}}\big)_{n}
,
\big(I_{d}-\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{u_{n}})\big)_{n}
and
\big(J^{\pm 1}_{u_{n}}-1\big)_{n}
to
I_{d}-A_{u}
,
I_{d}-\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{u})
, and
J^{\pm 1}_{u}-1
, respectively. In fact, these strong convergences hold in
L^{\infty}((0,T),L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
for all
p\in[2,\infty)
, given that the sequences are uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Additionally, the bound in (
4.20
) indicates that the sequence
(\nabla u_{n})_{n}
is bounded in
L^{16}((0,T),L^{8}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
, which implies that
(\nabla u_{n})_{n}
converges strongly to
\nabla u
in
L^{p}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
for all
p\in[2,8)
. We are then able to pass to the limit in (
4.19
) and conclude that the limit
u
is a weak solution of (
4.2
). The remaining step is to establish the following time continuity:

u\in\mathscr{C}([0,T],H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})),\,\text{ and }\,u_{t}\in\mathscr{C}([0,T],L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})).

This property can be proved by following the arguments in Step 4 of
section

3.2.3
and using (
4.9
). This completes the proof of
4.2
.
∎

4.3.
Estimates

In this section,, we derive the estimates for
I(v,v)
,
\mu(\rho_{0}J^{-1}_{v},c_{0})
and
\lambda(\rho_{0}J^{-1}_{v},c_{0})
used in the proof of
4.1
above. We first recall some estimates for the flow of a Lipschitz vector field. We refer to
[
5
, Lemma A.3]
for the proof.

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
v
be a vector field with
\nabla v\in L^{1}((0,T),L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
. Suppose that

c=\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}<1.

There exists a constant
k=k(c)
such that the following estimates hold true:

\begin{cases}\lVert I_{d}-\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v}(t));\;I_{d}-A_{v}(t)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}&\leqslant k\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{1}((0,t),L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))},\\
\lVert J_{v}^{\pm 1}(t)-1\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}&\leqslant k\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{1}((0,t),L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}.\end{cases}

Moreover, one has the following:

\begin{cases}\lVert\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v}(t))D_{A_{v}(t)}w-Dw\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}&\leqslant k\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{1}((0,t),L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\lVert Dw\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\\
\lVert\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v}(t))\dvg_{A_{v}(t)}w-\dvg wI_{d}\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}&\leqslant k\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{1}((0,t),L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\lVert Dw\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.\end{cases}

In the spirit of the above result, and owing to the fact that
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is an algebra,
one has the following estimates.

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
v
be a vector field fulfilling
\nabla v\in L^{1}((0,T),\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
. Suppose that

(4.23)

\displaystyle c=\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}<1.

There exists a constant
k=k(c)
such that the following estimates holds true:

\begin{cases}\lVert I_{d}-\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v}(t));\;I_{d}-A_{v}(t)\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}&\leqslant k\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{1}((0,t),\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))},\\
\lVert J_{v}^{\pm 1}(t)-1\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}&\leqslant k\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{1}((0,t),\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}.\end{cases}

Moreover, one has the following:

\begin{cases}\lVert\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v}(t))\mathbb{D}_{A_{v}(t)}w-\mathbb{D}w\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}&\leqslant k\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{1}((0,t),\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\lVert Dw\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\\
\lVert\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v}(t))\dvg_{A_{v}(t)}w-\dvg wI_{d}\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}&\leqslant k\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{1}((0,t),\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\lVert Dw\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.\end{cases}

Notice that we will need to estimate the first and the second time derivative of
I(v,v)
as it is required in the definition of
Y_{T}
. From (
2.6
) we have
\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\cdot D\mathscr{X}_{v}=\det(D\mathscr{X}_{v})I_{d}
and

\partial_{t}D\mathscr{X}_{v}=Dv
and consequently:

Lemma 4.6
.

The following expressions hold true:

(4.24)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}J^{-1}_{v}(t,y)=-J_{v}^{-1}(t,y)\dvg_{A_{v}}(v),

(4.25)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}A_{v}=-A_{v}\cdot Dv\cdot A_{v},

(4.26)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})=(Dv\colon\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v}))A_{v}-\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\cdot Dv\cdot A_{v}.

Next, taking the time derivative of the above quantities, one has:

(4.27)

\displaystyle J_{v}\partial_{tt}J^{-1}_{v}=(\dvg_{A_{v}}(v))^{2}-Dv_{t}\colon A_{v}+Dv\colon(A_{v}\cdot Dv\cdot A_{v}),

(4.28)

\displaystyle\partial_{tt}A_{v}=2A_{v}\cdot(Dv\cdot A_{v})^{2}-A_{v}\cdot Dv_{t}\cdot A_{v},

\displaystyle\partial_{tt}\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})

\displaystyle=(Dv_{t}\colon\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v}))A_{v}+J_{v}(Dv\colon A_{v})^{2}A_{v}-(Dv\colon(\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\cdot Dv\cdot A_{v}))A_{v}

(4.29)

\displaystyle-2(Dv\colon\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v}))A_{v}\cdot Dv\cdot A_{v}+2J_{v}A_{v}\cdot(Dv\cdot A_{v})^{2}-\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\cdot Dv_{t}\cdot A_{v}.

We now proceed to the estimates for
I(v,v)
,
\mu(\rho_{0}J^{-1}_{v},c_{0})
and
\lambda(\rho_{0}J^{-1}_{v},c_{0})
.

4.3.1.
Lebesgue and Hölder norm estimates for
I(v,v)

We first recall

(4.30)

\displaystyle I(v,v)=I_{1}(v,v)+I_{2}(v,v)+I_{3}(v)

where

(4.31)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}I_{1}(v,v)&=2\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\{\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\mathbb{D}_{A_{v}}v-\mathbb{D}v\},\\
I_{2}(v,v)&=\lambda(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\{\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\dvg_{A_{v}}v-\dvg v\},\\
I_{3}(v)&=\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\{P(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{P}\}I_{d}.\end{cases}

From
4.4
, we have the following estimates for the first two terms:

\lVert I_{1}(v,v);I_{2}(v,v)\rVert_{L^{m}_{T}L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{1}_{T}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{m}_{T}L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

for all
2\leqslant m,q\leqslant\infty
. Regarding the Hölder estimates, one has from
4.5
the following:

\displaystyle\lVert\sigma^{r/4}(I_{1}(v,v);I_{2}(v,v))\rVert_{L^{4}_{T}\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{1}_{T}\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lVert\sigma^{r/4}\nabla v\rVert_{L^{4}_{T}\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

It now remains the norm of
I_{3}(v)
. We may first write:

I_{3}(v)=\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\{P(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})-P(\rho_{0},c_{0})+P(\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{P}\}

and thus, we deduce from
4.4
, the following:

\lVert I_{3}(v)\rVert_{L^{m}_{T}L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim T^{1/m}\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{1}_{T}L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+T^{1/m}\lVert P(\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{P}\rVert_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

and finally, from
4.5
, we have the following Hölder estimate for
I_{3}(v)
:

\lVert\sigma^{r/4}I_{3}(v)\rVert_{L^{4}_{T}\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim T^{1/4}\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{1}_{T}\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}+T^{1/4}\lVert P(\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{P}\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

We gather all of these estimates in order to obtain:

\lVert I(v,v)\rVert_{L^{m}_{T}L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{1}_{T}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{m}_{T}L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+T^{1/m}\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{1}_{T}L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+T^{1/m}\lVert P(\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{P}\rVert_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

and

\lVert\sigma^{r/4}I(v,v)\rVert_{L^{4}_{T}\mathscr{C}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{1}_{T}\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lVert\sigma^{r/4}\nabla v\rVert_{L^{4}_{T}\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+T^{1/4}\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{1}_{T}\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\\
+T^{1/4}\lVert P(\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{P}\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

This proves (
4.6
), (
4.7
), and (
4.8
), and we now move on to estimating the time derivative of
I(v,v)
.

4.3.2.
Lebesgue norm estimates for
\partial_{t}I(v,v)

In this step, we will estimate the
L^{2}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
of
\partial_{t}I(v,v)
and the
L^{8/d}_{T}L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
norm of
\sigma^{\tfrac{d}{4}}\partial_{t}I(v,v)
. Let us begin by computing the time derivative of the third term
I_{3}(v)
. We have:

(4.32)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}I_{3}(v)=\partial_{t}\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\{P(J_{v}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{P}\}+\rho_{0}\partial_{\rho}P(J_{v}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\partial_{t}J^{-1}_{v}

and thus one obtains from (
4.24
) and (
4.26
) the following:

(4.33)

\displaystyle\lVert\partial_{t}I_{3}(v)\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\left(\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{1}_{T}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+1\right).

On the other hand, the following estimate holds true for
\partial_{t}I_{3}(v)
:

\displaystyle\lVert\sigma^{\tfrac{d}{4}}\partial_{t}I_{3}(v)\rVert_{L^{8/d}_{T}L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{8/d}_{T}L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\left(\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{1}_{T}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+1\right).

We turn to the estimate of the norms of
\partial_{t}I_{1}(v,v)
and
\partial_{t}I_{2}(v,v)
. First, we begin by writing:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}I_{1}(v,v)

\displaystyle=2\rho_{0}\partial_{\rho}\mu(J_{v}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})\partial_{t}J_{v}^{-1}\{\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\mathbb{D}_{A_{v}}v-\mathbb{D}v\}

\displaystyle+2\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\{\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\mathbb{D}_{A_{v}}v_{t}-\mathbb{D}v_{t}\}

\displaystyle+2\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\partial_{t}\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\mathbb{D}_{A_{v}}v

(4.34)

\displaystyle+2\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\left(Dv\cdot\partial_{t}A_{v}+\partial_{t}A^{T}_{v}\cdot\nabla v\right).

Using
4.4
and the expression of the time derivative of
J_{v}^{-1}
(
4.24
), the following bounds hold for the first term of the expression above:

\displaystyle\lVert 2\rho_{0}\partial_{\rho}\mu(J_{v}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})\partial_{t}J_{v}^{-1}\{\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\mathbb{D}_{A_{v}}v-\mathbb{D}v\}\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{1}_{T}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{4}_{T}L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

and

\displaystyle\lVert\sigma^{\tfrac{d}{4}}\rho_{0}\partial_{\rho}\mu(J_{v}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})\partial_{t}J_{v}^{-1}\{\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\mathbb{D}_{A_{v}}v-\mathbb{D}v\}\rVert_{L^{8/d}_{T}L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{1}_{T}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{16/d}_{T}L^{8}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

The same argument helps us to control the second term of the right hand side of (
4.34
) as follows:

\lVert 2\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\{\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\mathbb{D}_{A_{v}}v_{t}-\mathbb{D}v_{t}\}\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{1}_{T}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lVert\nabla v_{t}\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

and

\lVert\sigma^{\tfrac{d}{4}}\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\{\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\mathbb{D}_{A_{v}}v_{t}-\mathbb{D}v_{t}\}\rVert_{L^{8/d}_{T}L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{1}_{T}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lVert\sigma^{\tfrac{d}{4}}\nabla v_{t}\rVert_{L^{8/d}_{T}L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Next, from the expression of the time derivative of
A_{v}
, (
4.25
), we have:

\displaystyle Dv\cdot\partial_{t}A_{v}+\partial_{t}A^{T}_{v}\cdot\nabla v

\displaystyle=-(Dv\cdot A_{v})^{2}-(A_{v}^{T}\cdot\nabla v)^{2}

thus, the others terms of (
4.34
) are controlled as follows:

\displaystyle\lVert\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\partial_{t}\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\mathbb{D}_{A_{v}}v;\;\,

\displaystyle\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\{Dv\cdot\partial_{t}A_{v}+\partial_{t}A^{T}_{v}\cdot\nabla v\}\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{4}_{T}L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

and

\displaystyle\lVert\sigma^{\tfrac{d}{4}}\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\partial_{t}\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\mathbb{D}_{A_{v}}v;\;

\displaystyle\sigma^{\tfrac{d}{4}}\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\{Dv\cdot\partial_{t}A_{v}+\partial_{t}A^{T}_{v}\cdot\nabla v\}\rVert_{L^{8/d}_{T}L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{16/d}_{T}L^{8}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

Gathering all of these computations and noticing that the estimate for
\partial_{t}I_{1}(v,v)
and
\sigma^{\tfrac{d}{4}}I_{1}(v,v)
also holds for
\partial_{t}I_{2}(v,v)
and
\sigma^{\tfrac{d}{4}}I_{2}(v,v)
respectively, one has:

\displaystyle\lVert\partial_{t}I(v,v)\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\left(\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{4}_{T}L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\right)\left(\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{1}_{T}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+1\right)+\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{1}_{T}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lVert\nabla v_{t}\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

and

\lVert\sigma^{\tfrac{d}{4}}\partial_{t}I(v,v)\rVert_{L^{8/d}_{T}L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\left(\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{8/d}_{T}L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{16/d}_{T}L^{8}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\right)\left(\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{1}_{T}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+1\right)\\
+\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{1}_{T}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lVert\sigma^{\tfrac{d}{4}}\nabla v_{t}\rVert_{L^{8/d}_{T}L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

This leads to (
4.9
) and (
4.10
).

4.3.3.
L^{2}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
norm estimate for
\sigma\partial_{tt}I(v,v)

As above, we may begin by computing the second time derivative of
I(v,v)
. First, from (
4.32
), we have the following expression for
\partial_{tt}I_{3}(v)
:

\partial_{tt}I_{3}(v,v)=\partial_{tt}I_{3,1}+\partial_{tt}I_{3,2}

where

(4.35)

\displaystyle\partial_{tt}I_{3,1}=\partial_{tt}\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\{P(J_{v}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{P}\}+\rho_{0}\partial_{\rho}P(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\partial_{t}J^{-1}_{v}\partial_{t}\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})

and

\displaystyle\partial_{tt}I_{3,2}

\displaystyle=\rho_{0}^{2}\partial_{\rho}^{2}P(J_{v}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})(\partial_{t}J^{-1}_{v})^{2}\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})+\rho_{0}\partial_{\rho}P(J_{v}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})\partial_{t}J_{v}^{-1}\partial_{t}\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})

(4.36)

\displaystyle+\rho_{0}\partial_{\rho}P(J_{v}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})\partial_{tt}J_{v}^{-1}\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v}).

Using the expression of the time derivative of
J^{-1}_{v}
, (
4.24
) and of
\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})
, (
4.26
), the last term of (
4.35
) and the first two terms of the right hand side of (
4.36
) can be estimated as follows:

\displaystyle\lVert\sigma\rho_{0}\partial_{\rho}P(J^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})\partial_{t}J^{-1}_{v}\partial_{t}\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v}),\;\sigma\rho_{0}^{2}\partial_{\rho}^{2}P(J_{v}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})(\partial_{t}J^{-1}_{v})^{2}\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{4}_{T}L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

Owing to the expression of the second time derivative of
J^{-1}_{v}
(
4.27
) and
\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})
(
4.29
), the remainder terms of
\partial_{tt}I_{3}(v)
are estimated as follows:

\displaystyle\lVert\sigma\partial_{tt}\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\{P(J_{v}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{P}\},

\displaystyle\;\sigma\rho_{0}\partial_{\rho}P(J_{v}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})\partial_{tt}J_{v}^{-1}\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\lVert\sigma\nabla v_{t}\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{4}_{T}L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

Gathering all of these estimates, one has:

(4.37)

\displaystyle\lVert\sigma\partial_{tt}I_{3}(v)\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\lVert\sigma\nabla v_{t}\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{4}_{T}L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

and we turn to the
L^{2}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
norm estimate of
\sigma\partial_{tt}I_{1}(v,v)
. From (
4.34
), we express:

\partial_{tt}I_{1}(v,v)=\sum_{k}^{4}\partial_{tt}I_{1,k}(v,v)

where:

\displaystyle\partial_{tt}I_{1,1}(v,v)

\displaystyle=2(\rho_{0})^{2}\partial_{\rho}^{2}\mu(J_{v}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})(\partial_{t}J_{v}^{-1})^{2}\{\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\mathbb{D}_{A_{v}}v-\mathbb{D}v\}

\displaystyle+2\rho_{0}\partial_{\rho}\mu(J_{v}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})\partial_{tt}J_{v}^{-1}\{\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\mathbb{D}_{A_{v}}v-\mathbb{D}v\}

\displaystyle+2\rho_{0}\partial_{\rho}\mu(J_{v}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})\partial_{t}J_{v}^{-1}\{\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\mathbb{D}_{A_{v}}v_{t}-\mathbb{D}v_{t}\}

\displaystyle+2\rho_{0}\partial_{\rho}\mu(J_{v}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})\partial_{t}J_{v}^{-1}\partial_{t}\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\mathbb{D}_{A_{v}}v

\displaystyle+2\rho_{0}\partial_{\rho}\mu(J_{v}^{-1}\rho_{0},c_{0})\partial_{t}J_{v}^{-1}\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\{Dv\cdot\partial_{t}A_{v}+\partial_{t}A_{v}^{T}\cdot\nabla v\},

\displaystyle\partial_{tt}I_{1,2}(v,v)

\displaystyle=2\rho_{0}\partial_{\rho}\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\partial_{t}J^{-1}_{v}\{\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\mathbb{D}_{A_{v}}v_{t}-\mathbb{D}v_{t}\}

\displaystyle+2\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\{\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\mathbb{D}_{A_{v}}v_{tt}-\mathbb{D}v_{tt}\}

\displaystyle+2\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\partial_{t}\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\mathbb{D}_{A_{v}}v_{t}

\displaystyle+2\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\{\mathbb{D}v_{t}\cdot\partial_{t}A_{v}+\partial_{t}A_{v}^{T}\cdot\nabla v_{t}\},

\displaystyle\partial_{tt}I_{1,3}(v,v)

\displaystyle=2\rho_{0}\partial_{\rho}\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\partial_{t}J^{-1}_{v}\partial_{t}\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\mathbb{D}_{A_{v}}v+2\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\partial_{tt}\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\mathbb{D}_{A_{v}}v

\displaystyle+2\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\partial_{t}\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\{\mathbb{D}_{A_{v}}v_{t}+Dv\cdot\partial_{t}A_{v}+\partial_{t}A_{v}^{T}\cdot\nabla v\}

and finally,

\displaystyle\partial_{tt}I_{1,4}(v,v)

\displaystyle=2\rho_{0}\partial_{\rho}\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\partial_{t}J^{-1}_{v}\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\left(Dv\cdot\partial_{t}A_{v}+\partial_{t}A^{T}_{v}\cdot\nabla v\right)

\displaystyle+2\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\partial_{t}\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\left(Dv\cdot\partial_{t}A_{v}+\partial_{t}A^{T}_{v}\cdot\nabla v\right)

\displaystyle+2\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\adj(D\mathscr{X}_{v})\left(Dv_{t}\cdot\partial_{t}A_{v}+\partial_{t}A^{T}_{v}\cdot\nabla v_{t}+Dv\cdot\partial_{tt}A_{v}+\partial_{tt}A^{T}_{v}\cdot\nabla v\right).

Combining (
4.24
), (
4.25
), (
4.26
), (
4.27
) and (
4.29
) one has:

\displaystyle\lVert\sigma\partial_{tt}I_{1}(v,v)\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{1}_{T}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\left(\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{6}_{T}L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{3}+\lVert\sigma^{\tfrac{d}{4}}\nabla v_{t}\rVert_{L^{8/d}_{T}L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{8/(4-d)}_{T}L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\lVert\sigma\nabla v_{tt}\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)

\displaystyle+\lVert\sigma^{\tfrac{d}{4}}\nabla v_{t}\rVert_{L^{8/d}_{T}L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{8/(4-d)}_{T}L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{6}_{T}L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{3}.

Finally, the same estimate holds true for the
L^{2}_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
norm of
\sigma\partial_{tt}I_{2}(v,v)
and gathering (
4.37
) we obtain (
4.11
). We turn to the estimation of the norms of the viscosity.

4.3.4.
Lebesgue and Hölder norm estimate for viscosity

This step makes more sense to the estimate of the lower, upper bound and the Hölder regularity of the viscosity
\mu
and
\lambda
. With the help of

4.4
, we have the following estimates for
\mu

\begin{cases}\lVert\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{\mu}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}&\leqslant\lVert\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{\mu}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\lVert\partial_{\rho}\mu\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{1}_{T}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\\
\lVert\llbracket\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{T}\mathcal{C}_{0}}&\leqslant\lVert\llbracket\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{T}\mathcal{C}_{0}}+\lVert\partial_{\rho}\mu\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{1}_{T}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\end{cases}

and a similar estimates hold for
\lambda(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})
. Also, one has the following Hölder estimate:

\lVert\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{T}\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\leqslant\lVert\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\lVert\partial_{\rho}\mu\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{1}_{T}\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

The same estimate also holds for
\lambda(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})
and we obtain (
4.12
). We now turn to the estimate for the time derivative of the viscosity.

4.3.5.
Estimates for time derivative of the viscosity

We estimate the norm of the time derivative
of
\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})
and
\lambda(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})
as required in (
3.2
). First, we express:

(4.38)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})=-\rho_{0}\partial_{\rho}\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})J_{v}^{-1}\dvg_{A_{v}}(v),

and it transpires that:

\lVert\partial_{t}\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\rVert_{L^{m}_{T}L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{m}_{T}L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\quad\lVert\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{t}\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\lVert\sqrt{\sigma}\nabla v\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

A similar estimates hold true for the time derivative of
\lambda(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})
and implicitly (
4.13
)-(
4.14
) follow. We turn to the estimates of the norm of
\partial_{tt}\mu
and
\partial_{tt}\lambda
as required in (
3.2
).

4.3.6.
Estimates for the second time derivative of the viscosity

From (
4.38
), it is obvious that the second time derivative of
\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})

reads:

\displaystyle\partial_{tt}\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})

\displaystyle=\rho_{0}^{2}\partial^{2}_{\rho}\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})(J_{v}^{-1}\dvg_{A_{v}}(v))^{2}+\rho_{0}\partial_{\rho}\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})J_{v}^{-1}(\dvg_{A_{v}}(v))^{2}

\displaystyle-\rho_{0}\partial_{\rho}\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})J_{v}^{-1}\dvg_{A_{v}}v_{t}-\rho_{0}\partial_{\rho}\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})J_{v}^{-1}\partial_{t}A_{v}^{T}\colon\nabla v,

hence:

\lVert\sigma\partial_{tt}\mu(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\lVert\nabla v\rVert_{L^{4}_{T}L^{8}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\lVert\sigma\nabla v_{t}\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

A similar estimate holds true for the second time derivative of
\lambda(J^{-1}_{v}\rho_{0},c_{0})
. This leads to (
4.15
) and concludes the estimates used in
4.1
.

Appendix A
Quantitative Hölder estimates

This section contains two results concerning the regularity of even-order Riesz transform of discontinuous functions. The first one is a recent contribution by Gancedo and García-Juárez
[
11
]
. Roughly speaking, they established that these operators are continuous on the space of piecewise Hölder continuous functions, provided that the discontinuity surfaces
enjoy certain regularities. The second result, obtained in Hoff
[
14
]
for
d=2
, concerns the extension of discontinuous functions.

The quantitative Hölder estimates for even-order Riesz operator obtained by Gancedo and García-Juárez reads as follows:

Lemma A.1

(
[
11
,
12
]
)
.

Assume the hypotheses in
section

1.1.1
hold for
\mathcal{C}
and consider a singular integral operator
\mathcal{T}
:

(A.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(g)(x)=p.v.\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K(x-y)g(y)dy

where the kernel
K
is:

K(x)=\dfrac{P_{2l}(x)}{\lvert x\rvert^{d+2l}}.

Here,
P_{2l}
is a homogeneous polynomial in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
of degree
2l
,
l\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\}
.

•

L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-Estimate.

Given
\alpha^{\prime}\in(0,1)
and
q>d/\alpha^{\prime}
, there exists a constant
C=C(d,q,\alpha^{\prime})
such that for all

g\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{pw,\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
w\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{pw,\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
:

(A.2)

\displaystyle\lVert\mathcal{T}(gw)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(\lVert g\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{pw,\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\ell_{\varphi}^{-\tfrac{d}{q}}\lVert g\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)\lVert w\rVert_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+C\lVert g\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lVert w\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{pw,\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

•

\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-Estimate.
There exists a constant
C=C(d,\alpha)
such that for all
w\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
:

(A.3)

\displaystyle\lVert\mathcal{T}(w)\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant C\lVert w\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+C\left(1+\lvert\mathcal{C}\rvert\right)\lVert w\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\mathfrak{P}\big(\lVert\mathcal{C}\rVert_{\text{Lip}}+\left\lvert\mathcal{C}\right\rvert_{*}\big)\left|\mathcal{\mathcal{C}}\right|_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{1+\alpha}},

where
\mathfrak{P}
is a polynomial depending on
P_{2l}
.

For
d=2
,
A.1
summarizes the computations in Step 4 and Step 5 of
[
12
]
. It should be noted that the proof uses a geometric lemma derived in
[
1
]
for
d=2
. This extra cancellation property of even-order Riesz operators is also valid in higher dimensions. We refer to
[
25
, Section 3]
, and more precisely to the estimate of the term in equation (12). Let us now move on to the second result, which concerns
the extension of piecewise Hölder continuous functions.

Lemma A.2
.

Assume the hypotheses in
section

1.1.1
hold for
\mathcal{C}
and let
q\in[1,\infty]
. For
g\in L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
there exists
g^{e}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that
g^{e}=g
on
D
(or
D^{c}
)
and:

(A.4)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\lVert g^{e}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}&\leqslant C\lVert g\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\\
\lVert g-g^{e}\rVert_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}&\leqslant C\ell_{\varphi}^{1/q}\lVert\llbracket g\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{q}(\mathcal{C})},\\
\lVert g^{e}\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\lVert g-g^{e}\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}&\leqslant C\lVert g\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+C\lVert\llbracket g\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C})}\ell^{-\alpha}_{\varphi}.\end{cases}

The constant
C
above is independent from
q,\,\alpha
and
\mathcal{C}
.

For
d=2
, the lemma above does not differ from Lemma 5.2 in
[
14
]
, except for the dependence on the interface regularity. The proof for
d=3
follows almost the same lines.

Proof of
A.2
.

Given that
\mathcal{C}\in\mathscr{C}^{1+\alpha}
, for each
x=(x^{\prime},x_{d})\in\mathcal{C}

2
2

2

For
y=(y_{1},\dots,y_{d})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we use the notation
y=(y^{\prime},y_{d})
, where
y^{\prime}=(y_{1},\dots,y_{d-1})
.
there exist a neighborhood
W^{\prime}
of
x^{\prime}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d-1}
and a function
z\colon W^{\prime}\mapsto(x_{d}-\ell,x_{d}+\ell)\in\mathscr{C}^{1+\alpha}
such that:

\mathcal{C}\cap W=\{(y^{\prime},z(y^{\prime}))\colon y^{\prime}\in W^{\prime}\},\quad\text{ where }\quad W=W^{\prime}\times(x_{d}-\ell,x_{d}+\ell).

For all
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have:

\lvert\nabla\varphi(y)\rvert\geqslant\lvert\nabla\varphi(x)\rvert-\lvert\nabla\varphi(x)-\nabla\varphi(y)\rvert\geqslant\lvert\nabla\varphi(x)\rvert-\lvert x-y\rvert^{\alpha}\lVert\nabla\varphi\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}}.

Therefore, for
y
such that:

\lvert x-y\rvert^{\alpha}\lVert\nabla\varphi\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}}\leqslant\dfrac{1}{2}\lVert\nabla\varphi\rVert_{\text{inf}},

it follows that

\lvert\nabla\varphi(y)\rvert\geqslant\dfrac{1}{2}\lVert\nabla\varphi\rVert_{\text{inf}}.

In consequence, we can consider the local chart
(W^{\prime},z)
provided by the implicit function theorem, and
W^{\prime}
can be taken as
the
(d-1)
-dimensional ball centered at
x^{\prime}
with radius
\ell=C_{d}\ell_{\varphi}
. Furthermore, we might have:

(A.5)

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla z\rVert_{L^{\infty}(W^{\prime})}\leqslant 2.

We now cover
\mathcal{C}
with finite open bounded sets
W_{j}
,
j=2,\dots,N
and we set
W_{0}=D
and
W_{1}=\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\overline{D}
. For each
j=2,3,\dots,N
, we define the projection:

\displaystyle\vskip 5.69046pt\Xi_{j}\colon W_{j}\;

\displaystyle\;\to W_{j}\cap\mathcal{C}

\displaystyle x=(x^{\prime},x_{d})

\displaystyle\;\;\mapsto\Xi_{j}(x)=(x^{\prime},z_{j}(x^{\prime}))

and the local extension
g^{e}_{j}
:

(A.6)

\displaystyle g^{e}_{j}(x)=\begin{cases}g(x)&\text{if}\quad x\in W_{j}\cap\overline{D},\\
g(x)+\llbracket g\left(\Xi_{j}(x)\right)\rrbracket&\text{if}\quad x\in W_{j}\cap(\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\overline{D}).\end{cases}

Clearly,
g^{e}_{j}
is
\alpha
-Hölder continuous on
W_{j}\cap\overline{D}
and
W_{j}\cap(\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus D)
. However,
it is even more than that:
g_{j}^{e}
is
\alpha
-Hölder in the whole
W_{j}
with:

(A.7)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\lVert g_{j}^{e}-g\rVert_{L^{\infty}(W_{j})}&\leqslant\lVert\llbracket g\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}(W_{j}\cap\mathcal{C})},\\
\lVert g_{j}^{e}\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}(W_{j})}&\leqslant C\lVert g\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}}(W_{j})}.\end{cases}

Deriving
\eqref{c3.21}_{1}
, and obtaining the estimate of the piecewise Hölder norm for
g_{j}^{e}
is straightforward from (
A.6
). Next, we consider
x\in W_{j}\cap\overline{D}
and
y\in W_{j}\cap(\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\overline{D})
and we express:

\displaystyle\lvert g^{e}_{j}(x)-g^{e}_{j}(y)\rvert

\displaystyle=\lvert g(x)-g^{+}(y^{\prime},z_{j}(y^{\prime}))+g^{-}(y^{\prime},z_{j}(y^{\prime}))-g(y)\rvert

(A.8)

\displaystyle\leqslant\lVert g\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}}(W_{j})}\left(\lvert x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}\rvert^{\alpha}+\lvert x_{d}-z_{j}(y^{\prime})\rvert^{\alpha}+\lvert y_{d}-z_{j}(y^{\prime})\rvert^{\alpha}\right).

Recalling
y_{d}\leqslant z_{j}(y^{\prime})
and
z_{j}(x^{\prime})\leqslant x_{d}
, we have:

•

If
z_{j}(y^{\prime})\leqslant x_{d}
, then
y_{d}\leqslant z_{j}(y^{\prime})\leqslant x_{d}
and hence:

\lvert x_{d}-z_{j}(y^{\prime})\rvert+\lvert y_{d}-z_{j}(y^{\prime})\rvert\leqslant\lvert x_{d}-y_{d}\rvert.

•

If
z_{j}(y^{\prime})>x_{d}
, then
z_{j}(x^{\prime})\leqslant x_{d}\leqslant z_{j}(y^{\prime})
and using (
A.5
) we find:

\lvert x_{d}-z_{j}(x^{\prime})\rvert+\lvert x_{d}-z_{j}(y^{\prime})\rvert\leqslant\lvert z_{j}(x^{\prime})-z_{j}(y^{\prime})\rvert\leqslant 2\lvert x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}\rvert.

Whence:

\lvert y_{d}-z_{j}(y^{\prime})\rvert\leqslant\lvert y_{d}-x_{d}\rvert+\lvert x_{d}-z_{j}(y^{\prime})\rvert\leqslant 3\lvert x-y\rvert.

Summing up, (
A.8
) becomes:

\lvert g^{e}_{j}(x)-g^{e}_{j}(y)\rvert\leqslant C\lVert g\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}}(W_{j})}\lvert x-y\rvert^{\alpha}

and
\eqref{c3.21}_{2}
follows.

The global extension
g^{e}
reads:

(A.9)

\displaystyle g^{e}(x)=g(x)\left[\psi_{0}(x)+\psi_{1}(x)\right]+\sum_{j=2}^{N}g^{e}_{j}(x)\psi_{j}(x),

where
(\psi_{j})_{j}
is a partition of unity subordinate to the cover
(W_{j})_{j}
of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Given that
\psi_{j}
is compactly supported in
W_{j}
,
g\psi_{0}
,
g\psi_{1}
and
g^{e}_{j}\psi_{j}
,
j\geqslant 2
, extend over
\mathbb{R}^{d}
into
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
functions. Therefore,
g^{e}
itself belongs to
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and
\eqref{c3.16}_{1,2}
immediately holds for
q=\infty
from (
A.7
). From (
A.9
), we observe:

(A.10)

\displaystyle g^{e}(x)=g(x)+\sum_{j=2}^{N}\left(g^{e}_{j}(x)-g(x)\right)\psi_{j}(x),

and whence (recall (
A.6
) and the local finiteness of the cover):

g^{e}(x)=g(x)\quad\text{for}\quad x\in\overline{D}\quad\text{and}\quad\lVert g^{e}-g\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant C\lVert g\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+C\lVert\llbracket g\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C})}\sup_{j\geqslant 2}\lVert\psi_{j}\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Difference in (
A.9
) yields:

\displaystyle 2\big(g^{e}(x)-g^{e}(y)\big)

\displaystyle=\big(g(x)-g(y)\big)\big(\psi_{0}(x)+\psi_{1}(x)+\psi_{0}(y)+\psi_{1}(y)\big)

\displaystyle+\big(g(x)+g(y)\big)\sum_{j=0}^{j=1}\left(\psi_{j}(x)-\psi_{j}(y)\right)+\sum_{j=2}^{N}\left(g^{e}_{j}(x)-g_{j}^{e}(y)\right)\big(\psi_{j}(x)+\psi_{j}(y)\big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=2}^{N}\big(g_{j}^{e}(x)+g_{j}^{e}(y)\big)\left(\psi_{j}(x)-\psi_{j}(y)\right)

\displaystyle=\big(g(x)-g(y)\big)\big(\psi_{0}(x)+\psi_{1}(x)+\psi_{0}(y)+\psi_{1}(y)\big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=2}^{N}\left(g^{e}_{j}(x)-g_{j}^{e}(y)\right)\big(\psi_{j}(x)+\psi_{j}(y)\big)+\sum_{j=2}^{N}\big((g^{e}_{j}-g)(x)+(g^{e}_{j}-g)(y)\big)\left(\psi_{j}(x)-\psi_{j}(y)\right)

(A.11)

\displaystyle+\big(g(x)+g(y)\big)\sum_{j=0}^{N}\left(\psi_{j}(x)-\psi_{j}(y)\right),

where we notice that the last term vanishes.
This leads to

\lVert g^{e}\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant C\lVert g\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{pw,\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+C\lVert\llbracket g\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C})}\sup_{j}\lVert\psi_{j}\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}}

and
\eqref{c3.16}_{3}
follows since we have:

\sup_{j}\lVert\psi_{j}\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant C\ell^{-\alpha}.

We may observe that when
x\in D
and
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\overline{D}
, it follows that
\psi_{1}(x)=0\;\text{ and }\;\psi_{0}(y)=0
. Next, we choose
\widetilde{x}\in\mathcal{C}
(which implies
\psi_{0}(\widetilde{x})=\psi_{1}(\widetilde{x})=0
, since
\psi_{0}
and
\psi_{1}
vanish on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus D
and
\overline{D}
, respectively) such that

\lvert x-\widetilde{x}\rvert,\,\lvert y-\widetilde{x}\rvert\leqslant\lvert x-y\rvert.

Consequently, the first term of the right hand side of (
A.11
) shall be
expressed as follows:

\displaystyle\big(g(x)-g(y)\big)\big(\psi_{0}(x)+\psi_{1}(x)+\psi_{0}(y)+\psi_{1}(y)\big)

\displaystyle=\big(g(x)-g(y)\big)\big(\psi_{0}(x)+\psi_{1}(y)\big)

\displaystyle=\big(g(x)-g^{+}(\widetilde{x})+g^{-}(\widetilde{x})-g(y)\big)\big(\psi_{0}(x)+\psi_{0}(y)\big)

\displaystyle+\big(g^{+}(\widetilde{x})-g^{-}(\widetilde{x})\big)\big(\psi_{0}(x)-\psi_{0}(\widetilde{x})+\psi_{1}(y)-\psi_{1}(\widetilde{x})\big).

We now proceed with the proof of
\eqref{c3.16}_{2}
for
q\in[1,\infty)
. From (
A.10
) and Hölder’s inequality, we deduce:

\displaystyle\lVert g^{e}-g\rVert_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}

\displaystyle=\int_{D^{c}}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left(g^{e}_{j}(x)-g(x)\right)\psi_{j}(x)\right|^{q}dx

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{D^{c}}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{N}\psi_{j}(x)\right)^{q-1}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{N}\lvert g^{e}_{j}(x)-g(x)\rvert^{q}\psi_{j}(x)\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{j=1}^{N}\int_{D^{c}\cap W_{j}}\lvert g^{e}_{j}(x)-g(x)\rvert^{q}\psi_{j}(x)dx

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\ell\sum_{j=1}^{N}\int_{W_{j}^{\prime}}\lvert\llbracket g(\Xi_{j}(x^{\prime}))\rrbracket\rvert^{q}dx^{\prime}\quad(\text{recall}\;\;\eqref{c3.22})

\displaystyle\leqslant C\ell\sum_{j=1}^{N}\int_{W_{j}\cap\mathcal{C}}\lvert\llbracket g\rrbracket\rvert^{q}d\sigma

and
\eqref{c3.16}_{2}
follows. This concludes the proof of
A.2
.

Appendix B
Study of linear non homogeneous heat equation

This section addresses the well-posedness of the following system of PDE:

(B.1)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}v_{t}-\widetilde{\mu}\Delta v-(\widetilde{\mu}+\widetilde{\lambda})\nabla\dvg v=f+\dvg(b_{0}\cdot v),\\
v_{|t=0}=v_{0}.\end{cases}

The main result is presented in the following theorem.

Theorem B.1
.

Let
T\in(0,\infty)
,
v_{0}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
b_{0}\in W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
f\in L^{2}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then, the Cauchy problem (
B.1
) admits a unique solution
v\in\mathscr{C}([0,T],H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
. Moreover
v_{t},\,\nabla^{2}v\in L^{2}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

B.1.
Preliminary

We define

\mathcal{A}_{k}=\begin{cases}\mathcal{P}\quad\text{if}\quad k=\widetilde{\mu},\\
\mathcal{Q}\quad\text{if}\quad k=2\widetilde{\mu}+\widetilde{\lambda}\end{cases}

where
\mathcal{P}
(resp.
\mathcal{Q}
) is the projector onto the space of divergence-free (resp. curl-free) vector fields.
Applying
\mathcal{A}_{k}
to (
B.1
), we infer that the divergence-free and curl-free part of any regular
3
3

3

It is sufficient that
\partial_{t}v\in L^{2}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
v\in L^{2}((0,T),H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
.
solution
v
of (
B.1
) verify:

(B.2)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}_{k}v-k\Delta\mathcal{A}_{k}v=\mathcal{A}_{k}f+\mathcal{A}_{k}\dvg(b_{0}\cdot v),\\
\mathcal{A}_{k}v_{|t=0}=\mathcal{A}_{k}v_{0}.\end{cases}

Conversely, since
\mathcal{P}+\mathcal{Q}=I
, any regular vector field
v
that fulfills (
B.2
) is a solution of (
B.1
).

We shall now consider the following Cauchy problem

(B.3)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}_{k}v-k\Delta\mathcal{A}_{k}v=\mathcal{A}_{k}f+\mathcal{A}_{k}\dvg(b_{0}\,\colon w),\\
\mathcal{A}_{k}v_{|t=0}=\mathcal{A}_{k}v_{0},\end{cases}

where
w\in\mathscr{C}([0,T],H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
is a given vector field. The solution of this linear non-homogeneous heat equation is given by
the Duhamel formula:

(B.4)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{k}v(t)=\mathcal{T}(kt)\mathcal{A}_{k}v_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{T}(k(t-s))\mathcal{A}_{k}f(s)ds+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{T}(k(t-s))\mathcal{A}_{k}\dvg(b_{0}\,\colon w)(s)ds,

where
(\mathcal{T}(t))_{t}
is the heat semi-group. It follows
v\in\mathscr{C}([0,T],H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
with the following estimate:

(B.5)

\displaystyle\sup_{[0,T]}\lVert v\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant\lVert v_{0}\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\sum_{k}\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\mathcal{T}(k(t-s))\mathcal{A}_{k}\left[f(s)+\dvg(b_{0}\,\colon w)\right](s)\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}ds.

Additionally, based on
[
6
, Lemma 2.2]
, we infer that
\partial_{t}v,\,\nabla^{2}v\in L^{2}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
and that there exists a constant
C_{k}=C_{k}(k)>0
such that we have:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}_{k}v,\nabla^{2}\mathcal{A}_{k}v\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\leqslant C_{k}\lVert\mathcal{A}_{k}v_{0}\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+C_{k}\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\Delta\mathcal{T}(k(t-s))\mathcal{A}_{k}\left[f+\dvg(b_{0}\,\colon w)\right](s)ds\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}dt.

This therefore defines a map

\displaystyle\Phi\colon\,\;\mathscr{C}([0,T],H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))

\displaystyle\quad\to\quad\mathscr{C}([0,T],H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))

\displaystyle w

\displaystyle\quad\mapsto\quad\Phi(w)

where
\Phi(w)
is the unique solution of (
B.3
), which is given by (
B.4
) and fulfills (
B.5
). In the following we will set some tools in order to apply the Banach’s fixed-point theorem.

B.2.
Tools

We begin by the following Lemma:

Lemma B.2
.

There exists a constant
C=C\left(\lVert b\rVert_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)
such that:

\forall\;\varphi\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\quad\,\forall\,t>0\quad\lVert\mathcal{T}(kt)\mathcal{A}_{k}\dvg(b_{0}\,\colon\varphi)\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant C(kt)^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Proof.

We first write:

\mathcal{T}(kt)\mathcal{A}_{k}\dvg(b_{0}\,\colon\varphi)=G_{kt}*\mathcal{A}_{k}\dvg(b_{0}\,\colon\varphi)=\nabla G_{kt}*\mathcal{A}_{k}(b_{0}\,\colon\varphi)

where
G_{t}
is the heat kernel. Thanks to the continuity of
\mathcal{A}_{k}
on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and Young’s inequality we obtain:

\lVert\mathcal{T}(kt)\mathcal{A}_{k}\dvg(b_{0}\,\colon\varphi)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant\lVert\nabla G_{kt}\rVert_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lVert b_{0}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

where

\lVert\nabla G_{t}\rVert_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\dfrac{(4\pi t)^{-\tfrac{d}{2}}}{2t}(2\sqrt{t})^{d+1}\lvert\partial B(0,1)\rvert\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{d}e^{-r^{2}}dr.

Similarly, we obtain:

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla\mathcal{T}(kt)\mathcal{A}_{k}\dvg(b_{0}\,\colon\varphi)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\lVert\nabla G_{kt}\rVert_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lVert\nabla(b_{0}\,\colon\varphi)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\lVert\nabla G_{kt}\rVert_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lVert b_{0}\rVert_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

This concludes the proof of
B.2
.
∎

We now turn to the stability of equations (
B.4
) and to do this, we set

R_{k,T}=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|\mathcal{T}(kt)\mathcal{A}v_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{T}(k(t-s))\mathcal{A}_{k}f(s)ds\right\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\quad\text{and}\quad R_{T}=R_{\widetilde{\mu},T}+R_{2\widetilde{\mu}+\widetilde{\lambda},T}.

Lemma B.3
.

There exist constants
K>0
and
\beta_{0}>0
such that for all
\beta\geqslant\beta_{0}
the condition

(B.6)

\displaystyle\forall\,t\in[0,T],\quad\lVert w(t)\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant e^{\beta t}KR_{T}

implies the same for
\Phi(w)
, for all
w\in\mathscr{C}([0,T],H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
.

Proof.

From (
B.4
),
B.2
and assumption (
B.6
) on
w
, we have:

\displaystyle\lVert\mathcal{A}_{k}\Phi(w)(t)\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leqslant R_{k,T}+\int_{0}^{t}\lVert\mathcal{T}(k(t-s))\mathcal{A}_{k}\dvg(b_{0}\,\colon w)(s)\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}ds

\displaystyle\leqslant R_{k,T}+Ck^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}KR_{T}\int_{0}^{t}\dfrac{e^{\beta s}}{\sqrt{t-s}}ds.

For the condition (
B.6
) to be satisfied for
\Phi(w)
, it is sufficient that

(B.7)

\displaystyle 1+2C{\widetilde{\mu}}^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}K\int_{0}^{t}\dfrac{e^{\beta s}}{\sqrt{t-s}}ds\leqslant Ke^{\beta t}.

Owing to a change of variable, we have:

e^{-\beta t}\int_{0}^{t}\dfrac{e^{\beta s}}{\sqrt{t-s}}ds=\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{\beta}}\int_{0}^{\beta t}\dfrac{e^{-s}}{\sqrt{s}}ds\leqslant\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{\beta}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\dfrac{e^{-s}}{\sqrt{s}}ds=\sqrt{\dfrac{2}{\beta}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\tfrac{r^{2}}{2}}ds=\sqrt{\dfrac{\pi}{\beta}}

hence,

1+2C{\widetilde{\mu}}^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}K\int_{0}^{t}\dfrac{e^{\beta s}}{\sqrt{t-s}}ds\leqslant 1+2CKe^{\beta t}\sqrt{\dfrac{\pi}{\widetilde{\mu}\beta}}.

We choose
K
as

K=\dfrac{1}{4C}\sqrt{\dfrac{\beta_{0}\widetilde{\mu}}{\pi}}

with
\beta_{0}
large such that
K\geqslant 2
, and the condition (
B.7
) becomes

1+Ck^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}K\int_{0}^{t}\dfrac{e^{\beta s}}{\sqrt{t-s}}ds\leqslant 1+\dfrac{e^{\beta t}}{2}\sqrt{\dfrac{\beta_{0}}{\beta}}\leqslant Ke^{\beta t}.

This concludes the proof of
B.3
.
∎

Lemma B.4
.

There exists
\beta_{0}^{\prime}>0
such that
for all
\beta\geqslant\beta^{\prime}_{0}
and for all
v,\,w\in\mathscr{C}([0,T],H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
, one has:

(B.8)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-\beta t}\lVert\Phi(v)(t)-\Phi(w)(t)\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant\dfrac{1}{2}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-\beta t}\lVert v(t)-w(t)\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Proof.

Starting from (
B.4
) and
B.2
, one has:

\displaystyle\lVert\mathcal{A}_{k}\Phi(v)(t)-\mathcal{A}_{k}\Phi(w)(t)\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle=\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{T}(k(t-s))\mathcal{A}_{k}\dvg(b_{0}\,\colon(v-w))(s)ds\right\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leqslant Ck^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{t-s}}\lVert v(s)-w(s)\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}ds

\displaystyle\leqslant Ck^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}e^{-\beta s}\lVert v(s)-w(s)\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\int_{0}^{t}\dfrac{e^{\beta s}}{\sqrt{t-s}}ds.

It then turns out that,

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-\beta t}\lVert\mathcal{A}_{k}\Phi(v)(t)-\mathcal{A}_{k}\Phi(w)(t)\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant Ck^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\dfrac{\pi}{\beta}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-\beta t}\lVert v(t)-w(t)\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

and
B.4
follows by choosing
\beta^{\prime}_{0}>0
large enough.
∎

B.3.
Final step

We now turn to the application of the Banach’s fixed-point theorem in the following vector space

E:=\left\{v\in\mathscr{C}([0,T],H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))\colon\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-\beta t}\lVert v(t)\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}<\infty\right\}

endowed with the norm:

\forall\,v\in E,\quad\lVert v\rVert_{E}=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-\beta t}\lVert v(t)\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Above,
\beta
is a large number such that (
B.6
)-(
B.8
) hold true. It is obvious that
(E,\lVert\,\cdot\,\rVert_{E})
is a Banach space and, in particular the closed ball
E_{T}=B(0,KR_{T})
is complete with respect to the norm
\lVert\,\cdot\,\rVert_{E}
. On the first hand,
B.3
ensures that
\Phi
maps
E_{T}
into itself and the
B.4
ensures that
\Phi
is a contracting map. Hence, by Banach’s fixed-point theorem,
\Phi
admits a unique fixed-point
v

in
E_{T}
that solves the equation (
B.1
). Furthermore,
v
verifies

\mathcal{A}_{k}v=\mathcal{T}(kt)\mathcal{A}v_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{T}(k(t-s))\mathcal{A}_{k}f(s)ds+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{T}(k(t-s))\mathcal{A}_{k}\dvg(b_{0}\cdot v)(s)ds

and
[
6
, Lemma 2.2]
implies
v_{t},\nabla^{2}v\in L^{2}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Data availability

There are no data sets generated or analyzed in this study, therefore data sharing does not apply.
